0057               ; o TI-99/4A with 32k Memory Expansion

0063               ; When looking at this - remember that JUMP and BRANCH have the /opposite/
0064               ; meanings to the 6502 - JUMP is the short relative one, and BRANCH is absolute.

0066               ;
0067               ; CVBasic variables in scratchpad.
0068               ;
0069               
0070               ; don't warn on branch/jump optimizations or unused variables
0071               ;: warn-opts = off
0072               ;: warn-symbols = off

0074               ; use original (possibly incorrectly ported) random
0075      0000     OLD_RND equ 0
0076               
0077               ; We have to use our own workspace, not GPLWS, because the interrupt routine makes it unsafe to
0078               ; use r11, which we kind of need! So that eats 32 bytes of RAM but means most of the register
0079               ; restrictions I wrote this code on (avoid R1, avoid R6, avoid R13-R15) are lifted.
0080      8300     mywp      equ >8300
0081      8320     myintwp   equ >8320
0082               
0083               ; data storage in scratchpad
0084                   dorg >8340

0087      8340     firstsp         equ $

0091 8344          pletter_off	    bss 2       ; Used by Pletter

0094 8346          joy1_data	    bss 1       ; keep these bytes together and even aligned
0095 8347          joy2_data	    bss 1

0098 8349          key2_data	    bss 1       ; byte - keyboard (not used)

0155               ; used to track scratchpad variables
0156                   even
0157      8356     lastsp              equ $
0158               
0159               ; While we don't mean to USE the console ROM, for interrupts we
0160               ; are forced to interface with some of it. We need these addresses
0161               ; to minimize what it does so we can maximize our use of scratchpad.
0162               ; While I'd like to use the cassette hook - requires only 10 instructions
0163               ; and only uses 6 words of scratchpad, we can't here because it
0164               ; loses the return address, meaning you can only use it if you
0165               ; know where your LIMI 2 is and interrupts are otherwise disabled. So
0166               ; we have to use the longer but more standard interrupt hook, which also
0167               ; reads VDP status for us (no choice).
0168               
0169      8379     intcnt              equ >8379   ; interrupt counter byte, adds 1 (from GPLWS r14) every frame
0170      837B     vdp_status          equ >837B   ; VDP status byte mirror
0171      83C2     intwsr1             equ >83c2   ; INT WS R1  - interrupt control flags - must be >8000
0172      83C4     intwsr2             equ >83c4   ; INT WS R2  - address of user interrupt routine (point to int_handler)
0173      83D6     intwsr11            equ >83d6   ; screen timeout counter - must be odd (init to 1, is inct every frame)
0174      83DA     intwsr13            equ >83da   ; INT WS R13 - used for interrupt call (word)
0175      83DC     intwsr14            equ >83dc   ; INT WS R14 - used for interrupt call (word)
0176      83DE     intwsr15            equ >83de   ; INT WS R15 - used for interrupt call (word)
0177      83F6     gplwsr11            equ >83f6   ; GPL WS R11 - return address to interrupt ROM (not used, but overwritten each int)
0178      83F8     gplwsr12            equ >83f8   ; GPL WS R12 - used for cassette test and interrupt hook test (zeroed each int)
0179      83FA     gplwsr13            equ >83fa   ; GPL WS R13 - used in my interrupt handler
0180      83FC     gplwsr14            equ >83fc   ; GPL WS R14 - flags used to detect cassette - must be >0108 (or at least >0020 clear)
0181      83FE     gplwsr15            equ >83fe   ; GPL WS R15 - base address of VDP for status read - must be >8C02
0182               
0183               ; Some hardware equates
0184      83C0     INTWS     equ >83C0     ; interrupt calling Workspace
0185      83E0     GPLWP     equ >83E0     ; we use this one
0186      8400     SOUND     equ >8400
0187      8800     VDPDATA   equ >8800
0188      8802     VDPSTATUS equ >8802
0189      8C00     VDPWDATA  equ >8c00
0190      8C02     VDPWADR   equ >8c02

     603E 1FB0

     604C A98C

0225               ; this will be chopped up and inserted into the first three banks
0226               ; we're still in bank 0 - this will result in a gap in the output binary
0227               ; that we can use to put the pieces together more easily.

     A01A 4000

0261

     A032 8C02

     A03E 8800

0272               
0273               ; Fill VRAM - address in R0, byte in R2, count in R3

0275               ; Inline address set to avoid needing to cache r11

     A044 4000

     A054 8C00

0319               
0320               ; Define a pattern three times with 2k offsets - used for bitmap color and pattern tables
0321               ; Load VRAM 3 times with offset - address in R0, CPU data at R2, count in R3
0322               ; Original: address in pointer, CPU address at temp, count in temp2
0323               LDIRVM3
0324 A090 C10B  18     mov r11,r4      ; save return address
0325 A092 C142  18     mov r2,r5       ; save CPU address
0326 A094 C1C3  18     mov r3,r7       ; save count
0327 A096 06A0  32     bl @LDIRVM
     A098 A076     
0328 A09A 0220  22     ai r0,>0800     ; the OR'd mask doesn't matter
     A09C 0800     
0329 A09E C085  18     mov r5,r2       ; restore CPU
0330 A0A0 C0C7  18     mov r7,r3       ; restore count
0331 A0A2 06A0  32     bl @LDIRVM
     A0A4 A076     
0332 A0A6 0220  22     ai r0,>0800     ; the OR'd mask doesn't matter
     A0A8 0800     
0333 A0AA C085  18     mov r5,r2
0334 A0AC C0C7  18     mov r7,r3
0335 A0AE C2C4  18     mov r4,r11      ; for tail recursion
0336 A0B0 10E2  14     jmp LDIRVM

0356               ; copy a set of blocks of data to VDP, offset by 32 bytes each
0357               ; address in R8, CPU data at R9, count per row in R6 (MSB), number rows in R4 (MSB), CPU stride in R5 (MSB) (VDP stride fixed at 32)
0358               ; original: address in pointer, CPU address at temp, count per row in temp2, num rows in temp2+1, stride in YYXX
0359               CPYBLK
0360 A0D6 C1CB  18     mov r11,r7      ; save return
0361 A0D8 0986  32     srl r6,8
0362 A0DA 0984  32     srl r4,8
0363 A0DC 0985  32     srl r5,8        ; bytes to words
0364 A0DE 0300  22     limi 0
     A0E0 0000     
0365               !1
0366 A0E2 C008  18     mov r8,r0       ; get vdp address
0367 A0E4 C089  18     mov r9,r2       ; get cpu address
0368 A0E6 C0C6  18     mov r6,r3       ; get count
0369 A0E8 06A0  32     bl @LDIRVM      ; copy one row
     A0EA A076     
0370 A0EC A245  18     a r5,r9         ; add stride to CPU address
0371 A0EE 0228  22     ai r8,32        ; add 32 to VDP
     A0F0 0020     
0372 A0F2 0604  14     dec r4          ; count down rows
0373 A0F4 16F6  14     jne -!1         ; loop till done
0374 A0F6 0300  22     limi 2
     A0F8 0002     
0375 A0FA 0457  20     b *r7           ; back to caller
0376               
0377               ; clear screen and reset cursor to >1800
0378               cls
0379 A0FC C10B  18     mov r11,r4      ; save return
0380 A0FE 0200  20     li r0,>1800     ; SIT address
     A100 1800     
0381 A102 C800  30     mov r0,@cursor  ; save cursor
     A104 8342     
0382 A106 0202  20     li r2,>2000     ; byte to write
     A108 2000     
0383 A10A 0203  20     li r3,768       ; number of bytes
     A10C 0300     
0384 A10E 0300  22     limi 0          ; ints off
     A110 0000     
0385 A112 06A0  32     bl @FILVRM      ; write them
     A114 A042     
0386 A116 0300  22     limi 2          ; ints back on
     A118 0002

0391               print_string

0393 A11E C020  30     mov @cursor,r0  ; get cursor pos
     A120 8342     
0394 A122 0240  22     andi r0,>07ff   ; enforce position - pretty large range though? 80 column support maybe?
     A124 07FF     
0395 A126 0220  22     ai r0,>1800     ; add is safer than OR, and we have that option
     A128 1800     
0396 A12A A803  30     a r3,@cursor    ; add the count to cursor (might as well do it now!)
     A12C 8342     
0397 A12E 0300  22     limi 0
     A130 0000     
0398 A132 06A0  32     bl @LDIRVM      ; do the write
     A134 A076

     A138 0002

0469               
0470               ; Load sprite definitions: Sprite number in R4, CPU data in R0, count of sprites in R5 (MSB)
0471               ; Original: pointer = sprite number, temp = CPU address, a = number sprites
0472               ; Note: sprites are all expected to be double-size 16x16, 32 bytes each, so sprite char 1 is character 4
0473               ; Sprite pattern table at >3800
0474               define_sprite
0475 A1DA C20B  18     mov r11,r8          ; save return
0476 A1DC C080  18     mov r0,r2           ; Source data.
0477 A1DE C004  18     mov r4,r0           ; VRAM target.
0478 A1E0 0A50  26     sla r0,5            ; sprite number times 32 for bytes
0479 A1E2 0220  22     ai r0,>3800         ; add VDP base
     A1E4 3800     
0480 A1E6 C0C5  18     mov r5,r3		; Length.
0481 A1E8 0983  32     srl r3,8            ; make int
0482 A1EA 0A53  26     sla r3,5            ; count times 32
0483 A1EC 0300  22     limi 0              ; ints off
     A1EE 0000     
0484 A1F0 06A0  32     bl @LDIRVM          ; do the copy
     A1F2 A076     
0485 A1F4 0300  22     limi 2              ; ints on
     A1F6 0002     
0486 A1F8 0458  20     b *r8               ; back to caller
0487               
0488               ; Load character definitions: Char number in R1, CPU data in R2, count in R3 (MSB)
0489               ; Original: pointer = char number, temp = CPU address, a = number chars
0490               ; Note this loads the pattern three times if in bitmap mode (MODE&0x04)
0491               ; Pattern table at >0000
0492               define_char
0493 A1FA C20B  18     mov r11,r8          ; save return
0494 A1FC C080  18     mov r0,r2           ; source data
0495 A1FE C004  18     mov r4,r0           ; move input to scratch
0496 A200 0A30  22     sla r0,3            ; char number times 8 (VDP base is 0, so already there)
0497 A202 C0C5  18     mov r5,r3
0498 A204 0983  32     srl r3,8            ; make word
0499 A206 0A33  22     sla r3,3            ; count times 8
0500 A208 D160  30     movb @mode,r5       ; get mode flags
     A20A 834E     
0501 A20C 0245  22     andi r5,>0800
     A20E 0800     
0502 A210 1607  14     jne !1              ; not in bitmap mode, do a single copy
0503               
0504 A212 0300  22     limi 0              ; ints off
     A214 0000     
0505 A216 06A0  32     bl @LDIRVM3         ; do the triple copy
     A218 A090     
0506 A21A 0300  22     limi 2              ; ints on
     A21C 0002     
0507 A21E 0458  20     b *r8               ; back to caller
0508               
0509               !1
0510 A220 0300  22     limi 0              ; ints off
     A222 0000     
0511 A224 06A0  32     bl @LDIRVM          ; do the single copy
     A226 A076     
0512 A228 0300  22     limi 2              ; ints on
     A22A 0002     
0513 A22C 0458  20     b *r8               ; back to caller
0514               
0515               ; Load bitmap color definitions: Char number in R1, CPU data in R2, count in R3 (MSB)
0516               ; Original: pointer = char number, temp = CPU address, a = number chars
0517               ; Note: always does the triple copy. Color table at >2000
0518               define_color
0519 A22E C20B  18     mov r11,r8          ; save return
0520 A230 C080  18     mov r0,r2           ; source data
0521 A232 C004  18     mov r4,r0           ; move input to scratch
0522 A234 0A30  22     sla r0,3            ; char number times 8
0523 A236 0220  22     ai r0,>2000         ; add base address
     A238 2000     
0524 A23A C0C5  18     mov r5,r3
0525 A23C 0983  32     srl r3,8            ; make word
0526 A23E 0A33  22     sla r3,3            ; count times 8
0527 A240 0300  22     limi 0              ; ints off
     A242 0000     
0528 A244 06A0  32     bl @LDIRVM3         ; do the triple copy
     A246 A090     
0529 A248 0300  22     limi 2              ; ints on
     A24A 0002     
0530 A24C 0458  20     b *r8               ; back to caller
0531               
0532               ; Update sprite entry - copy data (4 bytes) to sprite table mirror at sprites
0533               ; R4 = sprite number, R5 = byte 1, r6 = byte 2, r7 = byte 3, r0 = byte 4 (all MSB)
0534               ; Original: A = sprite number, source data at sprite_data
0535               update_sprite
0536 A24E 0984  32     srl r4,8            ; make word
0537 A250 0A24  20     sla r4,2            ; x4 for address
0538 A252 0224  22     ai r4,sprites       ; sprite mirror address
     A254 2000     
0539 A256 DD05  32     movb r5,*r4+        ; move bytes
0540 A258 DD06  32     movb r6,*r4+        ; move bytes
0541 A25A DD07  32     movb r7,*r4+        ; move bytes
0542 A25C DD00  32     movb r0,*r4+        ; move bytes
0543 A25E 045B  20     b *r11
0544               
0545               ; SGN R0 - return 1, -1 or 0 as 16 bit
0546               _sgn16
0547 A260 C000  18     mov r0,r0       ; check for zero
0548 A262 1306  14     jeq !1          ; if yes, we're done
0549 A264 0240  22     andi r0,>8000   ; check for negative
     A266 8000     
0550 A268 1302  14     jeq !2          ; was not
0551 A26A 0700  14     seto r0         ; was negative, make it -1
0552 A26C 045B  20     b *r11          ; back to caller
0553               !2
0554 A26E 0580  14     inc r0          ; we know it was zero, and we want 1
0555               !1
0556 A270 045B  20     b *r11          ; back to caller
0557               
0558               ; 16-bit signed modulo. R1 % R2 = R0 - 9900 doesn't do signed divide
0559               ; original was stack%stack=YYAA
0560               ; Remainder is negative if the dividend was negative
0561               _mod16s
0562 A272 04C0  14     clr r0          ; make dividend 32-bit
0563 A274 C082  18     mov r2,r2       ; check divisor for zero
0564 A276 130A  14     jeq !1
0565 A278 0742  18     abs r2          ; make sure divisor is positive
0566 A27A C041  18     mov r1,r1       ; check sign of dividend
0567 A27C 1505  14     jgt !2          ; go do the faster positive version
0568               
0569 A27E 0741  18     abs r1          ; was negative, make it positive
0570 A280 3C02 128     div r2,r0       ; do the division => r2=quotient, r3=remainder
0571 A282 0501  14     neg r1          ; make remainder negative
0572 A284 C001  18     mov r1,r0       ; into r0
0573 A286 045B  20     b *r11
0574               
0575               !2
0576 A288 3C02 128     div r2,r0       ; do the division => r2=quotient, r3=remainder
0577 A28A C001  18     mov r1,r0       ; into r0
0578               !1
0579 A28C 045B  20     b *r11
0580               
0581               ; 16-bit signed divide. R1 / R2 = R0 - 9900 doesn't do signed divide
0582               ; original was stack/stack=YYAA
0583               ; Remainder is negative if the signs differ
0584               _div16s
0585 A28E 04C0  14     clr r0          ; make dividend 32-bit
0586 A290 C0C2  18     mov r2,r3       ; check divisor for zero
0587 A292 1308  14     jeq !1          ;
0588 A294 28C1  18     xor r1,r3
0589 A296 0742  18     abs r2
0590 A298 0741  18     abs r1          ; might as well make them positive now that we have copies
0591 A29A 3C02 128     div r2,r0       ; do the divide => r0=quotient, r1=remainder
0592 A29C 0243  22     andi r3,>8000   ; mask out sign bit
     A29E 8000     
0593 A2A0 1301  14     jeq !1          ; skip ahead to positive version
0594               
0595 A2A2 0500  14     neg r0          ; negate the result
0596               !1
0597 A2A4 045B  20     b *r11
0598               
0599               ; Random number generator - return in R0, (complex one uses R3,R4, simpler one only R0)
0600               ; Original output into YYAA
0601               random
0628               ; simpler one from dreamcast days...
0629 A2A6 C020  30     mov @lfsr,r0        ; get seed
     A2A8 834C     
0630 A2AA 0910  18     srl r0,1            ; shift
0631 A2AC 1702  14     jnc .rand1          ; jump if no 1
0632 A2AE 2820  34     xor @rmask,r0       ; xor new bits
     A2B0 A2B8     
0633               .rand1
0634 A2B2 C800  30     mov r0,@lfsr        ; save the output
     A2B4 834C     
0635 A2B6 045B  20     b *r11
0636               rmask
0637 A2B8 B400         data >b400          ; mask for 16 bit
0639               
0640               ; Set SN Frequency: R0=frequency code, R2=channel command (MSB)
0641               ; Original: A=least significant byte  X=channel command  Y=most significant byte
0642               sn76489_freq
0643 A2BA C0C0  18     mov r0,r3
0644 A2BC 0243  22     andi r3,>000f
     A2BE 000F     
0645 A2C0 06C3  14     swpb r3
0646 A2C2 F083  18     socb r3,r2
0647 A2C4 D802  30     movb r2,@SOUND  ; cmd and least significant nibble
     A2C6 8400     
0648 A2C8 0940  24     srl r0,4
0649 A2CA 0240  22     andi r0,>003f
     A2CC 003F     
0650 A2CE 06C0  14     swpb r0
0651 A2D0 D800  30     movb r0,@SOUND  ; most significant byte
     A2D2 8400     
0652 A2D4 045B  20     b *r11
0653               
0654               ; Set SN volume: R0=volume (MSB, inverse of attenuation), R2=channel command (MSB)
0655               ; Original: A=volume (inverse of attenuation), X=channel command
0656               sn76489_vol
0657 A2D6 0540  14     inv r0
0658 A2D8 0240  22     andi r0,>0f00
     A2DA 0F00     
0659 A2DC F002  18     socb r2,r0
0660 A2DE D800  30     movb r0,@SOUND
     A2E0 8400     
0661 A2E2 045B  20     b *r11
0662               
0663               ; Set noise type: R0=Noise type (MSB)
0664               ; original: A=noise command
0665               sn76489_control
0666 A2E4 0240  22     andi r0,>0f00
     A2E6 0F00     
0667 A2E8 0260  22     ori r0,>e000
     A2EA E000     
0668 A2EC D800  30     movb r0,@SOUND
     A2EE 8400     
0669 A2F0 045B  20     b *r11
0670               
0671               ; Set up vdp generic settings - R0 should be preloaded with a register in MSB, data in LSB
0672               ; R2 should contain the color table entry (in MSB), R3 the bitmap table (in MSB). Rest is
0673               ; hard coded. WARNING: Disables interrupts but does not re-enable them.
0674               vdp_generic_mode
0675 A2F2 C10B  18     mov r11,r4      ; save return
0676 A2F4 0300  22     limi 0          ; ints off
     A2F6 0000     
0677               
0678 A2F8 06A0  32     bl @WRTVDP      ; caller must set up this one
     A2FA A000     
0679 A2FC 0200  20     li r0,>01a2     ; VDP mode, screen off
     A2FE 01A2     
0680 A300 06A0  32     bl @WRTVDP
     A302 A000     
0681 A304 0200  20     li r0,>0206     ; >1800 pattern table
     A306 0206     
0682 A308 06A0  32     bl @WRTVDP
     A30A A000     
0683 A30C 0200  20     li r0,>0003     ; for color table
     A30E 0003     
0684 A310 F002  18     socb r2,r0
0685 A312 06C0  14     swpb r0
0686 A314 06A0  32     bl @WRTVDP
     A316 A000     
0687 A318 0200  20     li r0,>0004     ; for pattern table
     A31A 0004     
0688 A31C F003  18     socb r3,r0
0689 A31E 06C0  14     swpb r0
0690 A320 06A0  32     bl @WRTVDP
     A322 A000     
0691 A324 0200  20     li r0,>0536     ; >1b00 for sprite attribute table
     A326 0536     
0692 A328 06A0  32     bl @WRTVDP
     A32A A000     
0693 A32C 0200  20     li r0,>0607     ; >3800 for sprite pattern table
     A32E 0607     
0694 A330 06A0  32     bl @WRTVDP
     A332 A000     
0695 A334 0200  20     li r0,>0701     ; default screen color
     A336 0701     
0696 A338 06A0  32     bl @WRTVDP
     A33A A000     
0697 A33C 0454  20     b *r4
0698               
0699               ; set up VDP mode 0
0700               mode_0
0701 A33E C20B  18     mov r11,r8      ; careful - we call vdp_generic_mode and LDIRVM3
0702 A340 0200  20     li r0,>0800     ; bit we want to clear
     A342 0800     
0703 A344 5800  30     szcb r0,@mode
     A346 834E     
0704               
0705 A348 0202  20     li r2,>ff00	    ; $2000 for color table.
     A34A FF00     
0706 A34C 0203  20     li r3,>0300	    ; $0000 for bitmaps
     A34E 0300     
0707 A350 0200  20     li r0,>0002     ; r0 setting
     A352 0002     
0708 A354 06A0  32     bl @vdp_generic_mode    ; interrupts are now off
     A356 A2F2     
0709               
0710 A358 0200  20     li r0,>0100     ; target in VDP memory
     A35A 0100     
0711 A35C 0202  20     li r2,font_bitmaps  ; CPU memory source
     A35E A680     
0712 A360 0203  20     li r3,>0300     ; number of bytes
     A362 0300     
0713 A364 06A0  32     bl @LDIRVM3
     A366 A090     
0714               
0715 A368 0300  22     limi 2
     A36A 0002     
0716 A36C 0300  22     limi 0
     A36E 0000     
0717               
0718 A370 0200  20     li r0,>2000
     A372 2000     
0719 A374 0202  20     li r2,>f000
     A376 F000     
0720 A378 0203  20     li r3,>1800     ; fill color table with white on transparent
     A37A 1800     
0721 A37C 06A0  32     bl @FILVRM
     A37E A042     
0722               
0723 A380 0300  22     limi 2
     A382 0002     
0724 A384 06A0  32     bl @cls
     A386 A0FC     
0725 A388 C2C8  18     mov r8,r11      ; restore return address, and fall through to vdp_generic_sprites
0726               
0727               ; Initialize sprite table
0728               vdp_generic_sprites
0729 A38A C20B  18     mov r11,r8      ; save return address
0730 A38C 0200  20     li r0,>1b00     ; sprite attribute table in VDP
     A38E 1B00     
0731 A390 0202  20     li r2,>d100     ; off screen, and otherwise unimportant
     A392 D100     
0732 A394 0203  20     li r3,128       ; number of bytes
     A396 0080     
0733               
0734 A398 0300  22     limi 0
     A39A 0000     
0735 A39C 06A0  32     bl @FILVRM
     A39E A042     
0736               
0737 A3A0 0200  20     li r0,sprites
     A3A2 2000     
0738 A3A4 0202  20     li r2,>d1d1     ; write 2 bytes at a time
     A3A6 D1D1     
0739 A3A8 0203  20     li r3,128
     A3AA 0080     
0740               !1
0741 A3AC CC02  34     mov r2,*r0+     ; initialize CPU mirror
0742 A3AE 0643  14     dect r3
0743 A3B0 16FD  14     jne -!1
0744               
0745 A3B2 0200  20     li r0,>01e2     ; screen on
     A3B4 01E2     
0746 A3B6 06A0  32     bl @WRTVDP
     A3B8 A000     
0747               
0748 A3BA 0300  22     limi 2
     A3BC 0002     
0749 A3BE 0458  20     b *r8
0750               
0751               ; set up VDP mode 1
0752               mode_1
0753 A3C0 C20B  18     mov r11,r8      ; careful - we call vdp_generic_mode and LDIRVM3
0754 A3C2 0200  20     li r0,>0800     ; bit we want to clear
     A3C4 0800     
0755 A3C6 5800  30     szcb r0,@mode
     A3C8 834E     
0756               
0757 A3CA 0202  20     li r2,>ff00	    ; $2000 for color table.
     A3CC FF00     
0758 A3CE 0203  20     li r3,>0300	    ; $0000 for bitmaps
     A3D0 0300     
0759 A3D2 0200  20     li r0,>0002     ; r0 setting
     A3D4 0002     
0760 A3D6 06A0  32     bl @vdp_generic_mode    ; interrupts are now off
     A3D8 A2F2     
0761               
0762 A3DA 0200  20     li r0,>0000
     A3DC 0000     
0763 A3DE 0202  20     li r2,>0000
     A3E0 0000     
0764 A3E2 0203  20     li r3,>1800
     A3E4 1800     
0765 A3E6 06A0  32     bl @FILVRM      ; clear pattern table
     A3E8 A042     
0766               
0767 A3EA 0300  22     limi 2
     A3EC 0002     
0768               
0769 A3EE 0200  20     li r0,>2000
     A3F0 2000     
0770 A3F2 0202  20     li r2,>f000
     A3F4 F000     
0771 A3F6 0203  20     li r3,>1800
     A3F8 1800     
0772               
0773 A3FA 0300  22     limi 0
     A3FC 0000     
0774 A3FE 06A0  32     bl @FILVRM      ; init color table
     A400 A042     
0775 A402 0300  22     limi 2
     A404 0002     
0776               
0777 A406 0200  20     li r0,>5800     ; >1800 with the write bit set
     A408 5800     
0778 A40A 04C3  14     clr r3          ; value to write
0779               
0780               !1
0781 A40C 0300  22     limi 0          ; write the screen image table, but pause every 32 bytes for interrupts
     A40E 0000     
0782               
0783 A410 06C0  14     swpb r0
0784 A412 D800  30     movb r0,@VDPWADR
     A414 8C02     
0785 A416 06C0  14     swpb r0
0786 A418 D800  30     movb r0,@VDPWADR
     A41A 8C02     
0787               
0788 A41C 0202  20     li r2,32
     A41E 0020     
0789               
0790               !2
0791 A420 D803  30     movb r3,@VDPWDATA
     A422 8C00     
0792 A424 0223  22     ai r3,>0100
     A426 0100     
0793 A428 0602  14     dec r2
0794 A42A 16FA  14     jne -!2
0795               
0796 A42C 0300  22     limi 2
     A42E 0002     
0797 A430 0220  22     ai r0,32
     A432 0020     
0798 A434 0280  22     ci r0,>5b00
     A436 5B00     
0799 A438 1AE9  14     jl -!1
0800               
0801 A43A C2C8  18     mov r8,r11      ; restore return address
0802 A43C 10A6  14     jmp vdp_generic_sprites     ; using tail recursion
0803               
0804               ; Set up VDP mode 2
0805               mode_2
0806 A43E C20B  18     mov r11,r8      ; careful - we call vdp_generic_mode and LDIRVM3
0807 A440 0200  20     li r0,>0800     ; bit we want to set
     A442 0800     
0808 A444 F800  30     socb r0,@mode
     A446 834E     
0809               
0810 A448 0202  20     li r2,>8000	    ; $2000 for color table.
     A44A 8000     
0811 A44C 0203  20     li r3,>0000	    ; $0000 for bitmaps
     A44E 0000     
0812 A450 0200  20     li r0,>0000     ; r0 setting
     A452 0000     
0813 A454 06A0  32     bl @vdp_generic_mode    ; interrupts are now off
     A456 A2F2     
0814               
0815 A458 0200  20     li r0,>0100
     A45A 0100     
0816 A45C 0202  20     li r2,font_bitmaps
     A45E A680     
0817 A460 0203  20     li r3,>0300
     A462 0300     
0818 A464 06A0  32     bl @LDIRVM      ; load character set
     A466 A076     
0819               
0820 A468 0300  22     limi 2
     A46A 0002     
0821 A46C 0300  22     limi 0
     A46E 0000     
0822               
0823 A470 0200  20     li r0,>2000
     A472 2000     
0824 A474 0202  20     li r2,>f000
     A476 F000     
0825 A478 0203  20     li r3,>0020
     A47A 0020     
0826 A47C 06A0  32     bl @FILVRM      ; init color table
     A47E A042     
0827               
0828 A480 0300  22     limi 2
     A482 0002     
0829 A484 06A0  32     bl @cls         ; clear screen
     A486 A0FC     
0830 A488 C2C8  18     mov r8,r11      ; restore return
0831 A48A 0460  28     b @vdp_generic_sprites
     A48C A38A     
0832               
0833               ; this is where interrupts happen every frame
0834               ; Unlike a normal TI application, this one runs with interrupts ON,
0835               ; so all operations need to be sure to protect VDP address with LIMI 0,
0836               ; as well as any operations that might need to manipulate data managed
0837               ; by this interrupt. We enter via the normal user hook, so WP is on
0838               ; GPLWS, interrupts are off, and the VDP is already reset and status
0839               ; stashed on vdp_status (>837b). Our return address to the ROM is in
0840               ; r11, but we are NOT going to use it so that we don't need to reserve
0841               ; r8 for whatever nonsense it does. That means we need to load intws
0842               ; and RTWP ourselves at the end. We are on our own workspace so we
0843               ; don't have to worry about the main app's workspace.
0844               int_handler
0845               ; first copy the sprite table
0846 A48E 02E0  18     lwpi myintwp        ; separate safe workspace
     A490 8320     
0847               
0849 A492 C820  46     mov @>7ffe,@saved_bank  ; save bank switch page
     A494 7FFE     
     A496 8352     
0851               
0852 A498 020B  20     li r11,>005b        ; >1b00 with the write bit added, and byte flipped
     A49A 005B     
0853 A49C D80B  30     movb r11,@VDPWADR   ; SAL address
     A49E 8C02     
0854 A4A0 06CB  14     swpb r11
0855 A4A2 D80B  30     movb r11,@VDPWADR   ; going to copy the sprite table to VDP
     A4A4 8C02     
0856               
0857 A4A6 D2E0  30     movb @mode,r11
     A4A8 834E     
0858 A4AA 024B  22     andi r11,>0400      ; if bit >04 (inhibit flicker) is cleared, jump ahead to rotate
     A4AC 0400     
0859 A4AE 130A  14     jeq !4
0860               
0861 A4B0 04CB  14     clr r11             ; else we're going to just write it straight across
0862 A4B2 020C  20     li r12,128
     A4B4 0080     
0863 A4B6 020D  20     li r13,sprites
     A4B8 2000     
0864               !7
0865 A4BA D83D  40     movb *r13+,@VDPWDATA
     A4BC 8C00     
0866 A4BE 060C  14     dec r12
0867 A4C0 16FC  14     jne -!7
0868 A4C2 101B  14     jmp !5
0869               
0870               !4
0871 A4C4 D2E0  30     movb @flicker,r11   ; here we write it rotated every frame
     A4C6 834F     
0872 A4C8 022B  22     ai r11,>0400
     A4CA 0400     
0873 A4CC 024B  22     andi r11,>7f00
     A4CE 7F00     
0874 A4D0 D80B  30     movb r11,@flicker
     A4D2 834F     
0875 A4D4 06CB  14     swpb r11            ; make count
0876 A4D6 020C  20     li r12,32           ; count
     A4D8 0020     
0877               
0878               !6
0879 A4DA 022B  22     ai r11,sprites        ; this is still faster than separate incs
     A4DC 2000     
0880 A4DE D83B  40     movb *r11+,@VDPWDATA  ; copy one sprite
     A4E0 8C00     
0881 A4E2 D83B  40     movb *r11+,@VDPWDATA  ; no delay needed
     A4E4 8C00     
0882 A4E6 D83B  40     movb *r11+,@VDPWDATA
     A4E8 8C00     
0883 A4EA D81B  38     movb *r11,@VDPWDATA   ; small optimization, since we have an add coming anyway
     A4EC 8C00     
0884 A4EE 022B  22     ai r11,-(sprites-1)   ; remove address and add the rest of the increment
     A4F0 E001     
0885 A4F2 024B  22     andi r11,>007F        ; clamp it in range
     A4F4 007F     
0886 A4F6 060C  14     dec r12
0887 A4F8 16F0  14     jne -!6
0888               !5
0889               
0890               ; next read the joysticks - output needs to be 21xxLDRU - 1 and 2 are button and button2 respectively
0891               ; We don't have a button 2. We also need to read the keyboard and fill in key1_data. key2_data we
0892               ; will leave unused. Note key1_data expects Coleco-style 0-9,10-*,11-#,15=not pressed, but we can throw
0893               ; everything else as ASCII. We could do a split keyboard for 2 players, but I guess we'll leave it for now.
0894               ; joy1
0895 A4FA 020C  20     li r12,>0024    ; CRU base of select output
     A4FC 0024     
0896 A4FE 020D  20     li r13,>0600    ; joystick 1 column
     A500 0600     
0897 A502 30CD  30     ldcr r13,3      ; select it
0898 A504 0B7C  30     src r12,7       ; delay
0899 A506 020C  20     li r12,>0006    ; CRU base of return read
     A508 0006     
0900 A50A 360D  48     stcr r13,8      ; read 8 bits (we could get away with fewer, but be consistent)
0901 A50C 06A0  32     bl @convert_joystick
     A50E A5D4     
0902 A510 D80C  30     movb r12,@joy1_data
     A512 8346     
0903               
0904               ; joy2
0905 A514 020C  20     li r12,>0024    ; CRU base of select output
     A516 0024     
0906 A518 020D  20     li r13,>0700    ; joystick 2 column
     A51A 0700     
0907 A51C 30CD  30     ldcr r13,3      ; select it
0908 A51E 0B7C  30     src r12,7       ; delay
0909 A520 020C  20     li r12,>0006    ; CRU base of return read
     A522 0006     
0910 A524 360D  48     stcr r13,8      ; read 8 bits (we could get away with fewer, but be consistent)
0911 A526 06A0  32     bl @convert_joystick
     A528 A5D4     
0912 A52A D80C  30     movb r12,@joy2_data
     A52C 8347     
0913               
0914               ; do a quick modifier read for button 2 (control and fctn for joy 1 and 2 respectively)
0915 A52E 020C  20     li r12,>0024    ; CRU base of select output
     A530 0024     
0916 A532 04CD  14     clr r13         ; modifiers
0917 A534 30CD  30     ldcr r13,3      ; select it
0918 A536 0B7C  30     src r12,7       ; delay
0919 A538 020C  20     li r12,>0006    ; CRU base of return read
     A53A 0006     
0920 A53C 360D  48     stcr r13,8      ; read 8 bits (we could get away with fewer, but be consistent)
0921 A53E 020C  20     li r12,>4000    ; control
     A540 4000     
0922 A542 020B  20     li r11,>8000    ; button 2 bit
     A544 8000     
0923 A546 274C  18     czc r12,r13
0924 A548 1602  14     jne .noc1b2
0925 A54A F80B  30     socb r11,@joy1_data
     A54C 8346     
0926               .noc1b2
0927 A54E 020C  20     li r12,>1000    ; fctn
     A550 1000     
0928 A552 274C  18     czc r12,r13
0929 A554 1602  14     jne .noc2b2
0930 A556 F80B  30     socb r11,@joy2_data
     A558 8347     
0931               .noc2b2
0932               
0933               ; key1 - this is a very simple read with no modifiers, it just gives access to the letters and numbers
0934 A55A 04CB  14     clr r11         ; column
0935               !key1
0936 A55C 020C  20     li r12,>0024    ; CRU base of select output
     A55E 0024     
0937 A560 30CB  30     ldcr r11,3      ; select column
0938 A562 0B7C  30     src r12,7       ; delay
0939 A564 020C  20     li r12,>0006    ; CRU base of return read
     A566 0006     
0940 A568 360D  48     stcr r13,8      ; get the bits
0941 A56A 020C  20     li r12,7        ; bit search
     A56C 0007     
0942               !key2
0943 A56E 0A1C  18     sla r12,1
0944 A570 276C  34     czc @masktable(r12),r13    ; bit set?
     A572 A644     
0945 A574 1607  14     jne !key3       ; continue
0946 A576 091C  18     srl r12,1
0947 A578 095B  26     srl r11,5
0948 A57A A2CC  18     a r12,r11       ; calculate table offset
0949 A57C D82B  46     movb @keyboard_table(r11),@key1_data    ; might be a dead key, but that's okay
     A57E A614     
     A580 8348     
0950 A582 100D  14     jmp !key4
0951               
0952               !key3
0953 A584 091C  18     srl r12,1
0954 A586 060C  14     dec r12
0955 A588 15F2  14     jgt -!key2
0956 A58A 13F1  14     jeq -!key2      ; we don't have a jump if not negative
0957               
0958 A58C 022B  22     ai r11,>0100
     A58E 0100     
0959 A590 028B  22     ci r11,>0600
     A592 0600     
0960 A594 16E3  14     jne -!key1
0961               
0962 A596 020B  20     li r11,>0f00
     A598 0F00     
0963 A59A D80B  30     movb r11,@key1_data     ; no key was pressed
     A59C 8348     
0964               !key4
0965               
0966               ; check for quit - attempts to work it into the above were not working
0967               ; borrowed from console ROM
0968 A59E 020C  20     li r12,>0024
     A5A0 0024     
0969 A5A2 30CC  30     ldcr r12,3
0970 A5A4 0B7C  30     src r12,7
0971 A5A6 020C  20     li r12,>0006
     A5A8 0006     
0972 A5AA 360B  48     stcr r11,8
0973 A5AC 020C  20     li r12,>1100
     A5AE 1100     
0974 A5B0 26CC  18     czc r12,r11
0975 A5B2 1604  14     jne .noquit
0976 A5B4 04E0  26     clr @intwsr2
     A5B6 83C4     
0977 A5B8 0420  42     blwp @>0000
     A5BA 0000     
0978               .noquit
0979               
0986               
0987 A5BC 05A0  26     inc @frame
     A5BE 834A     
0988 A5C0 0200  20     li r0,3
     A5C2 0003     
0989 A5C4 A800  30     a r0,@lfsr  ; Make LFSR more random
     A5C6 834C     
0990               
1006               
1007               
1008               ; restore the saved bank
1010 A5C8 C020  30     mov @saved_bank,r0  ; recover page switch
     A5CA 8352     
1011 A5CC 04D0  26     clr *r0             ; switch it
1013               
1014               ; get back the interrupt workspace and return
1015 A5CE 02E0  18     lwpi INTWS
     A5D0 83C0     
1016 A5D2 0380  18     RTWP
1017               
1018               ; given a joystick read in r13, return bits in r12
1019               ; The final output is 8 bits:
1020               ; 21xxLDRU - 1 and 2 are button and button2 respectively
1021               ; NOTE: if called by the compiler, this won't act as expected
1022               convert_joystick
1023 A5D4 04CC  14     clr r12
1024 A5D6 2760  34     czc @joystick_table,r13
     A5D8 A60A     
1025 A5DA 1602  14     jne !j1
1026 A5DC 026C  22     ori r12,>0800
     A5DE 0800     
1027               !j1
1028 A5E0 2760  34     czc @joystick_table+2,r13
     A5E2 A60C     
1029 A5E4 1602  14     jne !j2
1030 A5E6 026C  22     ori r12,>0400
     A5E8 0400     
1031               !j2
1032 A5EA 2760  34     czc @joystick_table+4,r13
     A5EC A60E     
1033 A5EE 1602  14     jne !j3
1034 A5F0 026C  22     ori r12,>0200
     A5F2 0200     
1035               !j3
1036 A5F4 2760  34     czc @joystick_table+6,r13
     A5F6 A610     
1037 A5F8 1602  14     jne !j4
1038 A5FA 026C  22     ori r12,>0100
     A5FC 0100     
1039               !j4
1040 A5FE 2760  34     czc @joystick_table+8,r13
     A600 A612     
1041 A602 1602  14     jne !j5
1042 A604 026C  22     ori r12,>4000
     A606 4000     
1043               !j5
1044 A608 045B  20     b *r11
1045               
1046               joystick_table
1047 A60A 0200         data >0200,>0800,>0400,>1000,>0100    ; LDRU1
     A60C 0800     
     A60E 0400     
     A610 1000     
     A612 0100     
1048               
1049               ; By columns, then rows. 8 Rows per column. No shift states - converted to the Coleco returns
1050               ; for numbers, , and . become ; and #. Control is control1 button2, and Fctn is control2 button2
1051               keyboard_table
1052 A614 3D20         byte 61,32,11,15,254,15,255,15  ; '=',' ',enter,n/a,fctn,shift,ctrl,n/a
     A616 0B0F     
     A618 FE0F     
     A61A FF0F     
1053 A61C 2E4C         byte 46,76,79,9,2,83,87,88      ; '.','L','O','9','2','S','W','X'
     A61E 4F09     
     A620 0253     
     A622 5758     
1054 A624 2C4B         byte 44,75,73,8,3,68,69,67      ; ',','K','I','8','3','D','E','C'
     A626 4908     
     A628 0344     
     A62A 4543     
1055 A62C 4D4A         byte 77,74,85,7,4,70,82,86      ; 'M','J','U','7','4','F','R','V'
     A62E 5507     
     A630 0446     
     A632 5256     
1056 A634 4E48         byte 78,72,89,6,5,71,84,66      ; 'N','H','Y','6','5','G','T','B'
     A636 5906     
     A638 0547     
     A63A 5442     
1057 A63C 0A3B         byte 10,59,80,0,1,65,81,90      ; '/',';','P','0','1','A','Q','Z'
     A63E 5000     
     A640 0141     
     A642 515A     
1058               
1059               masktable
1060 A644 0100         data >0100,>0200,>0400,>0800,>1000,>2000,>4000,>8000
     A646 0200     
     A648 0400     
     A64A 0800     
     A64C 1000     
     A64E 2000     
     A650 4000     
     A652 8000     
1061 A654 0F00         data >0f00
1062               
1063               ; wait for frame to increment
1064               wait
1065 A656 C020  30     mov @frame,r0
     A658 834A     
1066               !1
1067 A65A 8800  30     c r0,@frame
     A65C 834A     
1068 A65E 13FD  14     jeq -!1
1069 A660 045B  20     b *r11
1070               
1071               ; initialize music system
1072               music_init
1073               ; mute sound chip
1074 A662 0200  20     li r0,>9fbf
     A664 9FBF     
1075 A666 D800  30     movb r0,@SOUND
     A668 8400     
1076 A66A 06C0  14     swpb r0
1077 A66C D800  30     movb r0,@SOUND
     A66E 8400     
1078 A670 0200  20     li r0,>dfff
     A672 DFFF     
1079 A674 D800  30     movb r0,@SOUND
     A676 8400     
1080 A678 06C0  14     swpb r0
1081 A67A D800  30     movb r0,@SOUND
     A67C 8400

1084               ; return if we don't have the player compiled in
1085 A67E 045B  20     b *r11

1928               
1929               ; The stack is simulated using R10. It's not used in
1930               ; these functions but the compiled code will need it.
1931               ; To push: dect r10, mov r0,*r10
1932               ; To pop:  mov *r10+,r0
1933               ; However, for JSR a function is helpful - call this like:
1934               ; bl @jsr
1935               ; data <function_address>
1936               jsr
1937 A980 C3BB  30     mov *r11+,r14       ; get the jump address
1938 A982 064A  14     dect r10            ; make room on stack
1939 A984 C68B  30     mov r11,*r10        ; save return address
1940 A986 069E  24     bl *r14             ; new subroutine call, we can come back here
1941 A988 C2FA  30     mov *r10+,r11       ; get real return off stack - warning, all basic functions do this inline rather than return
1942 A98A 045B  20     b *r11              ; back to caller
1943               
1944               ; entry code - we should enter with ints off anyway
1945               START
1946 A98C 0300  22     limi 0
     A98E 0000     
1947 A990 02E0  18     lwpi mywp           ; get our private workspace
     A992 8300     
1948 A994 020A  20     li R10,>4000        ; pseudo stack pointer
     A996 4000     
1949 A998 D820  42     movb @>8802,@vdp_status  ; clear any pending VDP interrupt and initialize vdp_status if needed
     A99A 8802     
     A99C 837B     
1950               
1951 A99E 0200  20     li r0,>0182         ; select 16k, magnified, blank, no ints
     A9A0 0182     
1952 A9A2 06A0  32     bl @wrtvdp          ; other ports write this twice... maybe to be 100% sure no NMI happens? We don't have that problem.
     A9A4 A000     
1953               
1954 A9A6 0200  20     li r0,firstsp       ; clear variables in scratchpad
     A9A8 8340     
1955               stlp1
1956 A9AA 04F0  30     clr *r0+
1957 A9AC 0280  22     ci r0,lastsp
     A9AE 8356     
1958 A9B0 1AFC  14     jl stlp1
1959               
1960 A9B2 0200  20     li r0,>2000         ; clear lower 8k RAM
     A9B4 2000     
1961               stlp2
1962 A9B6 04F0  30     clr *r0+
1963 A9B8 0280  22     ci r0,>4000
     A9BA 4000     
1964 A9BC 16FC  14     jne stlp2
1965               
1966 A9BE 0200  20     li r0,>0f0f
     A9C0 0F0F     
1967 A9C2 C800  30     mov r0,@key1_data   ; gets both - no key is >0f return
     A9C4 8348

     A9C8 A662

     A9CC A33E

     A9D0 A48E

     A9D4 83C4     
1974 A9D6 0200  20     li r0,>8000
     A9D8 8000     
1975 A9DA C800  30     mov r0,@intwsr1     ; disable most console ROM interrupt handling
     A9DC 83C2     
1976 A9DE 0201  20     li r1,1
     A9E0 0001     
1977 A9E2 C801  30     mov r1,@intwsr11    ; make sure screen timeout is odd so it never triggers
     A9E4 83D6

     A9E8 0108

     A9EC 83FC

     A9F0 8350

     A9F4 8C02

     A9F8 83FE

1984               ;;; CVBasic code starts here
1985               	; 	' ==========================================================================
1986               	; 	' KEYSTONE KAPERS -- CVBasic, dual target TI-99/4A + ColecoVision.
1987               	; 	'
1988               	; 	' Garry Kitchen's 1983 Activision game. See DESIGN.md; 0 records the
1989               	; 	' research and the four places the sources disagree.
1990               	; 	'
1991               	; 	' Fifty seconds to run Harry Hooligan down inside a department store eight
1992               	; 	' screens wide and four levels tall, before he reaches the roof.
1993               	; 	'
1994               	; 	' THE THREE DECISIONS THIS FILE IS BUILT ON, all from DESIGN.md:
1995               	; 	'
1996               	; 	'   * THE VIEW FLIPS, IT DOES NOT SCROLL. Eight discrete screens; crossing
1997               	; 	'     an edge blits the next one. The per-frame budget never pays for the
1998               	; 	'     store at all -- RallyX repaints 576 chars every camera cell and had
1999               	; 	'     to have all its movement rewritten to survive that.
2000               	; 	'   * A SCREEN IS EXACTLY 256 PX, so an actor's x within its screen is one
2001               	; 	'     unsigned byte. No 16-bit world coordinate exists in this program.
2002               	; 	'   * ZERO PER-FRAME VDP READS. An actor's floor is an index into flry(),
2003               	; 	'     never a question asked of the screen, so the Ms. Pac-Man trap
2004               	; 	'     (actors x per-actor search) is absent by construction.
2005               	; 	'
2006               	; 	' The rules that shaped the code, all from CLAUDE.md 3A, because every one
2007               	; 	' of them fails SILENTLY:
2008               	; 	'
2009               	; 	'   * A plain variable is 8-BIT and a CONST over 255 TRUNCATES. Every
2010               	; 	'     name-table offset past row 7 is a #var or a bare literal.
2011               	; 	'   * Every #var comparison is UNSIGNED.
2012               	; 	'   * `<cmp> AND <cmp>` is miscompiled by the 9900 backend. Nested IFs.
2013               	; 	'   * A sprite at y=208 TERMINATES the sprite list. Hidden sprites go 209.
2014               	; 	'   * A GOSUB left by GOTO never pops. Death, catch and escape are STATES
2015               	; 	'     set in a routine and acted on at the top of the main loop.
2016               	; 	'   * MPY clobbers r0. Nothing here multiplies into an index -- the tables
2017               	; 	'     tsrc()/bdst()/lv8() exist precisely so that no multiply is needed on
2018               	; 	'     a path that reads the result back.
2019               	; 	' ==========================================================================
2020               	;
2021               	; 	' ---------------------------------------------------------------- BANKING
2022               	; 	' THE ART AND THE STORE MAP LIVE IN ROM BANK 1 ON THE TI. The fixed area
2023               	; 	' is 24,336 bytes -- three 8,112-byte loader pages -- and unbanked this
2024               	; 	' program filled 24,304 of them, so there was no room left for anything at
2025               	; 	' all. `linkticart` does not warn when you exceed it; it silently discards
2026               	; 	' the top of the image, and what goes missing is whatever sits nearest the
2027               	; 	' end, usually a DATA block rather than code. Nothing errors.
2028               	; 	'
2029               	; 	' WHAT IS SAFE TO BANK. Everything moved here is read at SETUP (the
2030               	; 	' DEFINE CHAR / DEFINE SPRITE calls) or on a screen crossing (the template
2031               	; 	' blit and the escalator's phase tables) -- and, because there is exactly
2032               	; 	' ONE data bank and it is selected ONCE at startup and never switched, it
2033               	; 	' stays mapped for the life of the program. The rule in CLAUDE.md 3A is
2034               	; 	' about SWITCHING inside a frame or under the vblank ISR; with a single
2035               	; 	' permanently-selected bank there is no switch to miss. A missed
2036               	; 	' BANK SELECT would return bytes from the wrong page with no error at
2037               	; 	' build or run time, which is why there is only one and it is set before
2038               	; 	' anything reads from it.
2039               	; 	'
2040               	; 	' BANK ROM 128 sizes ColecoVision's Megacart mapper, NOT the TI cart --
2041               	; 	' that comes from how many bank files the assembler emits -- and it is
2042               	; 	' one of only four values the compiler accepts (128/256/512/1024).
2043               	; 	' The whole thing is gated on TI994A: the Coleco build is Z80 and roughly
2044               	; 	' half the size, so it neither needs banking nor wants to become a
2045               	; 	' Megacart image.
2046               	; 	#if TI994A
2047               	; 	BANK ROM 128
2048               	; 	#endif
2049               	; 	CONST SPRHID = 209		' NOT 208 -- 208 terminates the sprite list
2050      00D1     const_SPRHID	equ >00d1
2051               	;
2052               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------------------- geometry
2053               	; 	' A band is 5 rows: 4 rows of air (32 px) over one floor slab. Standing
2054               	; 	' Kelly is 16 px, so headroom is 16 px and the jump apex is 14 -- two
2055               	; 	' short of the ceiling, because an arc that bonks is silently truncated.
2056               	; 	' 4 px/frame = 240 px/s. At 2 it was not merely sluggish, it was
2057               	; 	' ARITHMETICALLY TOO SLOW: the climb is three traverses = 6,144 px, which
2058               	; 	' at 2 px/frame is 51 seconds of pure running against a 50-second clock,
2059               	; 	' before a single obstacle, escalator ride or catch. At 3 it is 34 s --
2060               	; 	' and 34 s was still wrong, for a reason a per-actor speed check cannot
2061               	; 	' see: HARRY'S CLIMB IS ALSO 34 s (DESIGN.md 4a). Kelly being 1.5x faster
2062               	; 	' bought nothing, because Harry's route is two thirds the length of
2063               	; 	' Kelly's -- he spawns beside floor 2's escalator and effectively skips a
2064               	; 	' traverse. A chase resolves on PATH / SPEED, not on speed, and the two
2065               	; 	' quotients were equal, so pursuit on foot could never close. At 4 the
2066               	; 	' climb is 25 s against Harry's 45, and the elevator buys back more.
2067               	; 	CONST WALKSP = 4		' px per frame
2068      0004     const_WALKSP	equ >0004
2069               	; 	CONST XWALL = 232		' furthest left edge at the store's east wall
2070      00E8     const_XWALL	equ >00e8
2071               	; 	CONST XWALW = 8			' nearest left edge at the west wall
2072      0008     const_XWALW	equ >0008
2073               	; 	CONST STANDH = 24		' Kelly standing -- TWO SPRITES tall
2074      0018     const_STANDH	equ >0018
2075               	; 	' KELLY DUCKED. It was 8 px, which is not a crouch -- it is a squash, with
2076               	; 	' no room to draw a figure bending in a DIRECTION. 11 px is, and the
2077               	; 	' biplane is raised to match (obh below, and the hitbox in coll_obst):
2078               	; 	' with the plane at 16..22 an 11 px crouch passes five pixels under it and
2079               	; 	' a 24 px standing Kop does not. The apex is 14, so a jump still cannot
2080               	; 	' clear it either -- the plane stays the one obstacle that must be ducked.
2081               	; 	'
2082               	; 	' AND 11 IS THE CEILING, not a preference. Duckable means the obstacle
2083               	; 	' clears the crouch, jumpable means it clears under the apex; raising the
2084               	; 	' crouch raises the duckable floor, so past 11 a band of beach-ball
2085               	; 	' heights is neither. A sweep of every crouch height against every ball
2086               	; 	' hitbox puts the limit at 11, and shrinking the ball does not move it --
2087               	; 	' the apex is what binds. assets/checkball.py fails the build on it.
2088               	; 	CONST DUCKH = 11		' Kelly ducked -- bent over, not squashed
2089      000B     const_DUCKH	equ >000b
2090               	; 	CONST CATCHR = 12		' catch / hit radius, centre to centre
2091      000C     const_CATCHR	equ >000c
2092               	;
2093               	; 	' Kelly states
2094               	; 	CONST ST_RUN = 0
2095      0000     const_ST_RUN	equ >0000
2096               	; 	CONST ST_JUMP = 1
2097      0001     const_ST_JUMP	equ >0001
2098               	; 	CONST ST_DUCK = 2
2099      0002     const_ST_DUCK	equ >0002
2100               	; 	CONST ST_ESC = 3		' riding an escalator -- no input, invincible
2101      0003     const_ST_ESC	equ >0003
2102               	; 	CONST ST_ELEV = 4		' inside the car     -- no input, invincible
2103      0004     const_ST_ELEV	equ >0004
2104               	;
2105               	; 	' obstacle kinds, matching assets/genstore.py
2106               	; 	CONST OB_CART = 1
2107      0001     const_OB_CART	equ >0001
2108               	; 	CONST OB_BALL = 2
2109      0002     const_OB_BALL	equ >0002
2110               	; 	CONST OB_RADIO = 3
2111      0003     const_OB_RADIO	equ >0003
2112               	; 	CONST OB_PLANE = 4
2113      0004     const_OB_PLANE	equ >0004
2114               	;
2115               	; 	' sprite patterns (DEFINE SPRITE index n -> pattern n*4)
2116               	; 	' THE VDP CANNOT MIRROR A SPRITE -- there is no flip bit -- so every actor
2117               	; 	' that runs both ways carries a second set of frames. assets/genart.py
2118               	; 	' generates the left ones from the right ones so the two directions are
2119               	; 	' guaranteed identical.
2120               	; 	' KELLY AND HARRY ARE 24 PX -- a 16 px top sprite with the top half of a
2121               	; 	' second sprite below it. A band gives 32 px of air plus the 7 px of the
2122               	; 	' ceiling row under its 1 px slab line = 39 px, and 24 + the 14 px jump is
2123               	; 	' 38. The apex CANNOT be reduced to buy room: at 10 px the ball's jump and
2124               	; 	' duck windows stop overlapping and a dead band opens (DESIGN.md 5a).
2125               	; 	'
2126               	; 	' The two sprites never share a scanline, so a band still costs Kelly 1 +
2127               	; 	' Harry 1 + two obstacles = four per line.
2128               	; 	' Pattern numbers are genart.py's SPRITES order x 4. Move one there and
2129               	; 	' this table must move with it -- nothing checks the correspondence.
2130               	; 	' SPRITE PATTERNS, laid out by genart.py so the selection code is three
2131               	; 	' statements rather than two mirrored branches. Everything that FACES comes
2132               	; 	' in a RIGHT block and then a LEFT block of the same shape, so changing
2133               	; 	' facing is one fixed offset; the LEGS do not face and are shared.
2134               	; 	CONST P_KHAT = 0		' Kelly RIGHT: hat, black
2135      0000     const_P_KHAT	equ >0000
2136               	; 	CONST P_KFACE = 4		'              face, skin
2137      0004     const_P_KFACE	equ >0004
2138               	; 	CONST P_KBODY = 8		'              tunic, arms down
2139      0008     const_P_KBODY	equ >0008
2140               	; 	CONST P_KBODYB = 12		'              tunic, leading arm up
2141      000C     const_P_KBODYB	equ >000c
2142               	; 	CONST P_KDHAT = 16		'              ducked: the brim, black
2143      0010     const_P_KDHAT	equ >0010
2144               	; 	CONST P_KDFACE = 20		'              ducked: face
2145      0014     const_P_KDFACE	equ >0014
2146               	; 	CONST P_KDBODY = 24		'              ducked: the crouch, blue
2147      0018     const_P_KDBODY	equ >0018
2148               	; 	CONST P_KFACING = 28		' add this for Kelly's LEFT set
2149      001C     const_P_KFACING	equ >001c
2150               	; 	' THE RUN IS FOUR FRAMES AND BOTH FACINGS SHARE THEM. In a side view "left
2151               	; 	' foot forward" is the horizontal mirror of "right foot forward", so the
2152               	; 	' cycle is A, B, mirror(A), mirror(B) and which way he is going lives in
2153               	; 	' the hat, face and tunic. Consecutive, so a frame is P_KLEG1 + 4*phase.
2154               	; 	CONST P_KLEG1 = 56
2155      0038     const_P_KLEG1	equ >0038
2156               	; 	CONST P_HBODY = 72		' Harry RIGHT: cap + body, white
2157      0048     const_P_HBODY	equ >0048
2158               	; 	CONST P_HFACE = 76		'              face, skin
2159      004C     const_P_HFACE	equ >004c
2160               	; 	CONST P_HSTRIPE = 80		'              the stripes, cap to hem
2161      0050     const_P_HSTRIPE	equ >0050
2162               	; 	CONST P_HBODYB = 84		'              torso, arms the other way
2163      0054     const_P_HBODYB	equ >0054
2164               	; 	' 16, NOT 12: the right-hand set grew a fourth entry when Harry got a
2165               	; 	' second torso frame, so the left set starts four patterns further on.

2287               	; 	' NAMED, because this was the literal 113 and the escalator rework
2288               	; 	' renumbered the character table underneath it. 113 became EXITC, so
2289               	; 	' the second collectible quietly drew an EXIT DOOR in the aisle -- a
2290               	; 	' plausible-looking box, no error, and nothing to connect it to a
2291               	; 	' change made somewhere else entirely.
2292               	; 	CONST CH_CASE = 153
2293      0099     const_CH_CASE	equ >0099
2294               	;
2295               	; 	' the elevator doorway, in pixels and columns
2296               	; 	CONST ELXL = 112
2297      0070     const_ELXL	equ >0070
2298               	; 	CONST ELXR = 143
2299      008F     const_ELXR	equ >008f
2300               	; 	CONST ELCOL = 14		' first of 4 doorway columns
2301      000E     const_ELCOL	equ >000e
2302               	; 	CONST ELWAIT = 100		' frames stopped at a floor
2303      0064     const_ELWAIT	equ >0064
2304               	; 	CONST ELMOVE = 45		' frames in transit between floors
2305      002D     const_ELMOVE	equ >002d
2306               	; 	CONST ELDOOR = 15		' frames the doors spend part-open
2307      000F     const_ELDOOR	equ >000f
2308               	; 	CONST ELOPEN = 85		' = ELWAIT - ELDOOR, as a literal: a CONST
2309      0055     const_ELOPEN	equ >0055
2310               	; 					' built from other CONSTs is exactly the
2311               	; 					' folded-constant trap in CLAUDE.md 3A
2312               	;
2313               	; 	' escalator boarding zones, in CENTRE-x pixels
2314               	; 	' ESCALATOR BOARDING ZONES, IN PIXELS, AND THEY TRACK THE ART.
2315               	; 	' The flight is drawn as four rows of two characters stepping two columns
2316               	; 	' per row: west runs cols 1-2 (top) down to 7-8 (foot), east runs 29-30
2317               	; 	' (top) down to 23-24 (foot). These zones ARE those columns x8. When the
2318               	; 	' escalator art changed from a filled triangle to a stepped flight the
2319               	; 	' foot moved, and a boarding zone left where it was would put the player
2320               	; 	' on a staircase that is not under them -- no error, just a floor that
2321               	; 	' teleports you when you walk over an empty patch of it.
2322               	; 	' THESE ARE THE DRAWN STEPS, in screen pixels. The flight is fifteen
2323               	; 	' characters wide: its foot tread sits at x 95-103 (west) and 152-160
2324               	; 	' (east), its head tread at 23-31 and 224-232. An actor rides between
2325               	; 	' those, so the numbers here are the ART's, and moving the art without
2326               	; 	' moving them puts the rider beside the escalator rather than on it.
2327               	; 	' THE RIDER STANDS ON A STEP, and everything about the ride follows from
2328               	; 	' WHICH ONE. The staircase is a straight line -- a step 4 px higher is 8
2329               	; 	' px further along it -- so one number, the step's height above the floor
2330               	; 	' being left, fixes both the rider's x and the rest of the ride:
2331               	; 	'
2332               	; 	'     west  klx = 99 - 2*height        east  klx = 140 + 2*height
2333               	; 	'
2334               	; 	' The bottom step is 4 px up, so a rider walking on boards at klx = 91
2335               	; 	' (west) or 148 (east); the head step is 40 up, so the ride ends at 19 or
2336               	; 	' 220. Those are the ART's numbers -- the foot tread is drawn at x 95-103
2337               	; 	' west and 152-160 east, the head tread at 23-31 and 224-232 -- so moving
2338               	; 	' the art without moving these puts the rider beside the staircase.
2339               	; 	'
2340               	; 	' THE PHASE MATTERS. The steps climb 1 px a frame and the animation runs
2341               	; 	' four 1 px phases, so the bottom step is 4 + escp px up, not 4. Boarding
2342               	; 	' without that term leaves the feet up to 3 px out of register with the
2343               	; 	' tread and the whole ride drifts, which is exactly what "he floats
2344               	; 	' beside the steps" looked like.
2345               	; 	CONST ESCFX = 91		' west foot: rider's x when boarding
2346      005B     const_ESCFX	equ >005b
2347               	; 	CONST ESCFXE = 148		' east foot
2348      0094     const_ESCFXE	equ >0094
2349               	; 	CONST ESCHX = 19		' west head: rider's x on arrival
2350      0013     const_ESCHX	equ >0013
2351               	; 	CONST ESCHXE = 220		' east head
2352      00DC     const_ESCHXE	equ >00dc
2353               	; 	CONST ESCRISE = 40		' floor to floor, and the ride's length
2354      0028     const_ESCRISE	equ >0028
2355               	;
2356               	; 	' TIMER UNITS PER KROOK, AND A UNIT IS NOT A SECOND. Measured off the
2357               	; 	' reference video (DESIGN.md 0m): the original counts 50 down to 0 and a
2358               	; 	' unit lasts 1.99 s, so a round is about 100 seconds. TICKFR below is
2359               	; 	' what makes it so here.
2360               	; 	CONST TIMEL = 50		' timer units per Krook
2361      0032     const_TIMEL	equ >0032
2362               	; 	' FRAMES PER TIMER UNIT. 120, not 60, and this is not cosmetic -- it is
2363               	; 	' what makes a round finishable. Everything that MOVES in this game is
2364               	; 	' paced per loop PASS, and the loop runs about 25 passes a second, so
2365               	; 	' Kelly's 4 px a pass is ~100 px/s (the reference's Kelly is 102, so the
2366               	; 	' speeds match). The timer, though, is paced by the frame DELTA -- real
2367               	; 	' wall-clock time. At 60 frames a unit the round was 50 real seconds
2368               	; 	' while Kelly's route to the roof takes about 72, so the clock ran out
2369               	; 	' before either of them could get there and every round ended on a
2370               	; 	' timeout. checkchase.py hid it by assuming 60 passes a second; it now
2371               	; 	' models the real rate.
2372               	; 	CONST TICKFR = 120
2373      0078     const_TICKFR	equ >0078
2374               	; 	' NINE TIMER UNITS -- not ten, and not nine seconds. Every source says a
2375               	; 	' cart, ball or radio costs "9 seconds", and what they mean is the number
2376               	; 	' on the HUD, because that is what the manual calls seconds. So the
2377               	; 	' DISPLAYED count drops by 9. A unit is about two real seconds
2378               	; 	' (DESIGN.md 0m), which makes one careless cart cost roughly eighteen --
2379               	; 	' close to a fifth of the round, and the reason the refractory latch
2380               	; 	' below matters so much.
2381               	; 	CONST HITPEN = 9		' timer units a cart / ball / radio costs
2382      0009     const_HITPEN	equ >0009
2383               	; 	CONST HITREF = 45		' frames before the SAME obstacle can charge
2384      002D     const_HITREF	equ >002d
2385               	; 					' again. See coll_obst -- clearing the latch
2386               	; 					' on the first non-overlapping frame is not
2387               	; 					' enough, because a bouncing ball leaves
2388               	; 					' contact between bounces.
2389               	;
2390               	; 	' ---------------------------------------------------------------- tables
2391               	; 	DIM flry(4)			' floor surface y, by level
2392               	; 	DIM #bdst(4)			' band name-table offset, by level
2393               	; 	DIM #tsrc(10)			' template source offset, by template id
2394               	; 	DIM lv8(4)			' lv*8, so no multiply lands on an index
2395               	; 	DIM jarc(32)			' the jump arc: 30 frames, apex 14
2396               	; 	DIM msk(8)
2397               	;
2398               	; 	' EIGHT obstacle slots -- TWO per band, and that number is forced.
2399               	; 	'
2400               	; 	' The VDP shows four sprites per scanline and DROPS the rest by slot
2401               	; 	' order; it does not flicker them, they simply are not there. Three per
2402               	; 	' band was sized against Kelly alone (1 + 3 = 4) and forgot that HARRY
2403               	; 	' can be on that band too -- and when he is, the fifth sprite on his
2404               	; 	' scanline is the highest-numbered obstacle, which vanishes while still
2405               	; 	' being solid. An invisible thing that costs nine seconds is the worst
2406               	; 	' failure in the game and it only happens when the crook is next to you,
2407               	; 	' which is exactly when you are not looking at the floor.
2408               	; 	'
2409               	; 	' Kelly (slot 0) + Harry (slot 1) + two obstacles = four. Provably never
2410               	; 	' dropped, on any band, in any situation.
2411               	; 	DIM obk(8)			' kind, 0 = empty
2412               	; 	DIM obx(8)			' x within the screen
2413               	; 	DIM obd(8)			' 0 = moving left, 1 = right
2414               	; 	DIM obs(8)			' speed
2415               	; 	DIM obh(8)			' art-bottom height above the slab
2416               	; 	DIM obc(8)			' cell column, radios only -- they are chars
2417               	; 	DIM obp(8)			' bounce phase, balls only
2418               	; 	DIM obht(8)			' hit refractory, per obstacle -- see coll_obst
2419               	;
2420               	; 	DIM cok(4)			' this screen's collectible, per band
2421               	; 	DIM coc(4)			' its column
2422               	; 	DIM takn(4)			' 32 bits: which bands have been cleaned out
2423               	;
2424               	; 	' ---------------------------------------------------------------- state
2425               	; 	' Kelly
2426               	; 	' klv 0..3 (0 = floor 1, 3 = roof), klsc 0..7, klx 0..255
2427               	; 	' Harry the same. NOTHING in this program is a world coordinate.
2428               	;

2580               	; setup:
2581               cvb_SETUP
2582               	; 	' Flicker stays OFF. CVBasic's is all-or-nothing -- it rotates all 32
2583               	; 	' slots, so Kelly would strobe too, and he is the one thing the player
2584               	; 	' must never lose. He is sprite 0 instead: the VDP drops the
2585               	; 	' HIGHEST-numbered sprites on an over-full scanline, so slot 0 is the
2586               	; 	' one slot that can never disappear.
2587               	; 	SPRITE FLICKER OFF
2588 AB16 0200  20 	li r0,>0400
     AB18 0400     
2589 AB1A F800  30 	socb r0,@mode
     AB1C 834E     
2590               	;
2591               	; 	' BEFORE ANY READ FROM IT, and never switched again. Bank 0 is the only
2592               	; 	' bank a BANK SELECT may be issued from, and this is the last thing that
2593               	; 	' runs before the DEFINEs below start pulling art out of bank 1.
2594               	; 	#if TI994A
2595               	; 	BANK SELECT 1
2596 AB1E 04E0  34 	clr @>6006
     AB20 6006

2634               	; 	' A flight crossing into the roof shows the grey-and-white DECK behind
2635               	; 	' it; one crossing into a shop floor shows the yellow FLOOR BAR. Same

2637               	; 	' third, because a roof crossing is screen row 5 and a shop one is row 10

     AD72 2231     
2862               	;
2863               	; 	' THE JUMP ARC IS A TABLE, not integration. That makes the 14 px apex a
2864               	; 	' property of the data rather than of a fixed-point velocity that has to
2865               	; 	' be tuned, and it keeps every comparison in the jump 8-bit and unsigned.
2866               	; 	' DESIGN.md 5a depends on the apex being exactly 14.
2867               	; 	'
2868               	; 	' THIRTY FRAMES WITH NINE OF THEM AT THE APEX. The first version was 24
2869               	; 	' frames that touched 14 for only four, which made every jump a timing
2870               	; 	' test rather than a decision -- you had to leave the ground on exactly
2871               	; 	' the right frame or clip the thing you were jumping. Widening the
2872               	; 	' plateau rather than raising the apex keeps the ball arithmetic in
2873               	; 	' DESIGN.md 5a intact (the apex is what that depends on) while making the
2874               	; 	' window forgiving: the player still has to CHOOSE to jump, but no longer
2875               	; 	' has to be frame-perfect about it.
2876               	; 	' THE ARC IS A TABLE, not thirty assignments -- each of those compiled to
2877               	; 	' several bytes of code, and the fixed area is the binding budget.
2878               	; 	#jat = VARPTR jarc_tbl(0)
2879 AD74 0200  20 	li r0,cvb_JARC_TBL
     AD76 7148     
2880 AD78 C800  38 	mov r0,@cvb__JAT
     AD7A 20A6     
2881               	; 	FOR ji = 0 TO 29
2882 AD7C 04C0  14 	clr r0
2883 AD7E D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_JI
     AD80 21A0     
2884               cv17
2885               	; 		#jaa = #jat + ji
2886 AD82 D020  34 	movb @cvb_JI,r0
     AD84 21A0     
2887 AD86 0980  32 	srl r0,8
2888 AD88 C040  18 	mov r0,r1
2889 AD8A C020  34 	mov @cvb__JAT,r0
     AD8C 20A6     
2890 AD8E A001  18 	a r1,r0
2891 AD90 C800  38 	mov r0,@cvb__JAA
     AD92 2098     
2892               	; 		jarc(ji) = PEEK(#jaa)
2893 AD94 D050  26 	movb *r0,r1
2894 AD96 D020  34 	movb @cvb_JI,r0
     AD98 21A0     
2895 AD9A 0980  32 	srl r0,8
2896 AD9C 0220  22 	ai r0,array_JARC
     AD9E 2206

2930 ADF2 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
2931 ADF4 0450  20 	b *r0
2932               	;
2933               	; 	' THE FONT'S COLOUR TABLE WAS 472 BYTES OF ONE REPEATED VALUE, which is a
2934               	; 	' fifth of what the fixed area had left. DEFINE COLOR copies such a table
2935               	; 	' out of ROM into all three screen thirds; writing the constant straight
2936               	; 	' into the colour table does the same job for the price of a loop, and
2937               	; 	' the loop runs once, at boot.
2938               	; 	'
2939               	; 	' The colour table is at >2000 and the VDP mirrors it once per screen
2940               	; 	' third, >800 apart. Both numbers were read out of the GENERATED assembly
2941               	; 	' -- define_color's `ai r0,>2000` and LDIRVM3's `ai r0,>0800` -- rather
2942               	; 	' than assumed, because a wrong base here would paint over the pattern
2943               	; 	' table and the failure would look like corrupt artwork.
2944               	; 	'
2945               	; 	' Paced: a few hundred VDP writes in one frame are silently dropped.
2946               	; 	' THE SCANNER'S COLOURS ARE THREE BLOCKS REPEATED SIXTEEN TIMES EACH, so
2947               	; 	' the table shipped 384 bytes to say 24 bytes' worth. Same trick as the
2948               	; 	' font: write the colour table directly. See font_colour for where >2000
2949               	; 	' and the >800 mirror stride come from.
2950               	; scan_colour:
2951               cvb_SCAN_COLOUR
2952               	; 	#scb = VARPTR scan_col3(0)
2953 ADF6 0200  20 	li r0,cvb_SCAN_COL3
     ADF8 6A9A     
2954 ADFA C800  38 	mov r0,@cvb__SCB
     ADFC 20A0

     AE02 21BD

3326               	even
3327               cv62
3328               	;
3329               	; 	' ------------------------------------------- ALPHA LOCK, CALIBRATED
3330               	; 	' On the TI, ALPHA LOCK shares a line with the joystick's VERTICAL axis.
3331               	; 	' Latched down it reports a direction that is NEVER RELEASED. Every other
3332               	; 	' game in this repo dodges this by not reading up/down at all; this one
3333               	; 	' cannot, because down is the duck and up is the elevator.
3334               	; 	'
3335               	; 	' THE FIRST VERSION OF THIS REFUSED TO START UNTIL THE AXIS CLEARED, AND
3336               	; 	' THAT WAS THE WRONG CALL. Classic99 defaults to invertcaps=1, so the TI
3337               	; 	' sees ALPHA LOCK DOWN when the host's Caps Lock is UP -- the normal
3338               	; 	' state. The game then sat on its title screen for ever waiting for a
3339               	; 	' condition the player had no reason to suspect, which presents as "the
3340               	; 	' title comes up and it will not start". A check that turns a survivable
3341               	; 	' input quirk into a dead game is worse than no check.
3342               	; 	'
3343               	; 	' So: sample the axis for 40 frames BEFORE any input can reasonably have
3344               	; 	' been given. A direction held for essentially all of them is not a player
3345               	; 	' -- it is the key. Record it, say so, and then IGNORE that direction for
3346               	; 	' the whole game, which makes ALPHA LOCK harmless instead of fatal.
3347               	; 	alku = 0
3348 B1EA 04C0  14 	clr r0
3349 B1EC D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_ALKU
     B1EE 214B     
3350               	; 	alkd = 0
3351 B1F0 04C0  14 	clr r0
3352 B1F2 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_ALKD
     B1F4 2144     
3353               	; 	alkn = 0
3354 B1F6 04C0  14 	clr r0
3355 B1F8 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_ALKN
     B1FA 214A

     B1FE A980

3945               	; 	' HARRY STARTS ON THE PLAYER'S SCREEN, one floor up. The research says

3948               	; 	' if he is four screens away. On a scrolling display those two facts are

4114               	;
4115               	; 	' HARRY'S SPEED IS IN QUARTER PIXELS, AND IT DOES NOT RAMP. It used to be
4116               	; 	' 2 px/frame against Kelly's 3, which sounds like a comfortable 1.5x --
4117               	; 	' and Kelly still lost the race to the roof by ELEVEN SECONDS, every
4118               	; 	' round, because the two routes are not the same length. Kelly runs
4119               	; 	' 7,992 px (three traverses plus the roof); Harry runs 4,104, because he
4120               	; 	' spawns beside floor 2's escalator and so skips a traverse. A chase
4121               	; 	' resolves on PATH / SPEED, and Kelly needs to be more than TWICE
4122               	; 	' Harry's speed before he is ahead at all. See DESIGN.md 4a.
4123               	; 	'
4124               	; 	' 1.75, AND THE NUMBER IS MEASURED, NOT GUESSED. Tracked off the
4125               	; 	' reference video (DESIGN.md 4a), the 2600's Kelly covers 0.40 screen
4126               	; 	' widths a second and its Harry 0.193 -- a ratio of 2.07. At 1.5 ours
4127               	; 	' was 2.67 and the crook visibly strolled. 1.75 brings it to 2.29.
4128               	; 	'
4129               	; 	' 2.0 would match the original exactly and still cannot be used, because
4130               	; 	' OUR routes are not the original's: Kelly's is 1.95x Harry's here, so a
4131               	; 	' speed ratio of 2.0 leaves him 5.2 s ahead at the escape edge -- less
4132               	; 	' than one beach ball (9 s). At 1.75 he is 10.1 s ahead, which absorbs a
4133               	; 	' hit. That figure counts the LIFT, which is on his way and carries him
4134               	; 	' two floors; the foot-only route is 5 s worse and is not the route
4135               	; 	' anybody takes.
4136               	; 	'
4137               	; 	' THE PER-KROOK DIAL IS STILL NOT HIS SPEED. It is the obstacles, which
4138               	; 	' is also the only dial the manual names (`obsp` above, and the ball
4139               	; 	' arcs). assets/checkchase.py checks all of this mechanically -- run it
4140               	; 	' if you touch these numbers.

4261               	;
4262               	; 	' A SUPPORT BEAM HAS TO REACH THE FLOOR IT HOLDS UP, AND THAT FLOOR IS
4263               	; 	' NOT IN ITS OWN BAND. A beam fills its band's four air rows, so its top
4264               	; 	' pixel sits under the slab row of the band ABOVE -- and a slab row is
4265               	; 	' five pixels of bar over three of green (see SLAB), so the beam stopped
4266               	; 	' three pixels short of the bar on every storey. It read as a beam that
4267               	; 	' misses the floor, which is exactly what it was.
4268               	; 	'
4269               	; 	' It cannot be drawn into either template. The beam belongs to the band
4270               	; 	' below and the row belongs to the band above, and the two templates are
4271               	; 	' chosen independently per screen, so no template knows both. So the
4272               	; 	' band blits go down first and the tops are stamped afterwards, from a
4273               	; 	' per-template column table -- the same shape as the escalator's head cap.
4274               	; 	'
4275               	; 	' Bands 0 and 1 only. Band 2's top row is the ROOF DECK, which is grey
4276               	; 	' over grey with no green to bridge, and the roof itself has no band
4277               	; 	' above it at all.

5447               cv264
5448               	; 		' HE IS STANDING ON A STEP, so he moves at the STEP's rate: 2 px
5449               	; 		' across and 1 up PER PASS -- the staircase's own slope, and exactly
5450               	; 		' what one phase of esc_tick moves the pattern by.
5451               	; 		'
5452               	; 		' NOT `fdv`, WHICH IS THE WHOLE POINT. Everything else in this loop
5453               	; 		' is paced by the frame delta, and pacing him that way too made him
5454               	; 		' outrun the steps the moment a pass cost more than one frame: his
5455               	; 		' feet started planted on a tread and ended floating above it.
5456               	; 		' Pacing the ANIMATION by fdv instead is worse -- four phases cannot
5457               	; 		' be stepped by 2 or 3 without aliasing, and the staircase runs
5458               	; 		' backwards (see esc_tick). The only arrangement that cannot drift
5459               	; 		' is the one where both are driven by the same fixed step, so the
5460               	; 		' rider is clocked by the pass, like the steps he is standing on.
5461               	; 		'
5462               	; 		' THE FIRST FEW PASSES ARE THE STEP ON. Walking on, his feet are on
5463               	; 		' the floor and the bottom tread is 4 to 7 px above them, so he
5464               	; 		' climbs at 2 px a pass until he catches it (the tread is rising at
5465               	; 		' 1, so the gap closes at 1 a pass) and then rides with it. Jumping
5466               	; 		' on he lands ON a tread, so eson is 0 and this never runs.
5467               	; 		IF esy < eson THEN esy = esy + 1

5769               	;
5770               	; 	' --- running
5771               	; 	' THE END WALLS ARE MOVEMENT LIMITS, NOT A CLAMP APPLIED AFTERWARDS.
5772               	; 	'
5773               	; 	' They used to be the latter, and that is the whole bug: on screen 7 Kelly
5774               	; 	' walked on to x = 254, the crossing routine then noticed there was no
5775               	; 	' screen 8 and set him to 232 -- a 22 px snap BACKWARDS. He reached the
5776               	; 	' wall and was thrown off it. Testing the limit BEFORE the step means he
5777               	; 	' arrives at the wall and stays there, which is what a wall does.
5778               	; 	' IN THE AIR HE IS BALLISTIC. The horizontal direction is fixed at
5779               	; 	' take-off and the stick is ignored until he lands: steering in mid-air
5780               	; 	' is not a jump, it is flight, and it also let him flip which way he was
5781               	; 	' facing halfway through an arc. kjdx is 0 for a standing jump, 1 for a
5782               	; 	' running one to the right, 2 to the left.
