6098               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------------------ escalator
6099               	; 	' ONE working escalator per floor, at that floor's alternating end --
6100               	; 	' west, east, west. An escalator drawn where none works would be a step
6101               	; 	' the player runs to and nothing happens.
6102               	; 	' ESCALATORS RUN BOTH WAYS. Stand at the foot and you are carried up;
6103               	; 	' stand at the head of the flight that arrives from the floor below and
6104               	; 	' you are carried down. It is one staircase used from either end, not two
6105               	; 	' -- which is also why the down zone on a floor is the UP zone of the
6106               	; 	' floor beneath it, offset by one band.
6107               	; 	' UP ONLY. Riding down was built at one point and has been taken out
6108               	; 	' again at the reviewer's direction -- these only ever climb.
6109               	; 	'
6110               	; 	' Losing it also removed the re-board lock, which existed solely because
6111               	; 	' stepping off at the head put you inside the zone that rode the same
6112               	; 	' flight back down. With no down ride there is no such zone: a floor's
6113               	; 	' escalator is at the opposite end from the one below it, so arriving at
6114               	; 	' the top of one never lands you on the foot of another.

6674               	; 	' THE VICTROLA, AS CHARACTERS. It never moves, so a sprite spent a
6675               	; 	' scan-line slot on something that can be painted once when the screen is
6676               	; 	' drawn -- and at one 16x16 sprite it could only ever be one colour and
6677               	; 	' one size. Four cells is twice the area for no sprite at all.
6678               	; 	'
6679               	; 	' It stands ON the slab, so it fills the band's rows 2 and 3; row 4 is
6680               	; 	' the floor bar itself and draw_prizes uses row 3 for a collectible,
6681               	; 	' which is why the two never share a screen (genstore keeps prizes off

7503               	;
7504               	; 	' THE QUARTER-PIXEL ACCUMULATOR. hx is one unsigned byte per screen (2),
7505               	; 	' so there is nowhere to keep a fraction -- the fraction lives here and
7506               	; 	' is spent as whole pixels. hsp4 = 6 walks 1,2,1,2 (1.5 px/frame); 7
7507               	; 	' walks 1,2,2,2 over four frames (1.75); 8 is a flat 2. Two IFs, not a
7508               	; 	' loop and not a divide: hacc is under 4 on entry and hsp4 is at most 8,
7509               	; 	' so it can never need a third. `%` and `/` both compile to a real
7510               	; 	' TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A) and this runs every frame.
7511               	; 	'
7512               	; 	' 7, NOT 6, AND THE REASON IS MEASURED. Tracked off the reference video
7513               	; 	' (DESIGN.md 4a), the 2600's Kelly covers 0.40 screen widths a second and
7514               	; 	' its Harry 0.193 -- a ratio of 2.07. At hsp4 = 6 ours was 2.67, and the
7515               	; 	' crook visibly strolled. 7 brings it to 2.29. 8 would match the original
7516               	; 	' exactly and cannot be used: Kelly's route here is 1.95x Harry's, so a
7517               	; 	' speed ratio of 2.0 leaves the on-foot chase 0.2 s of slack, which is no
7518               	; 	' chase at all. checkchase.py holds the line.
7519               	; 	' STILL RUNNING FROM THE STAIRS -- and this has to come BEFORE the move,
7520               	; 	' not after it. It sat below the two `IF hmv` blocks, where all it could
7521               	; 	' still reach was the animation counter: he faced the right way, played
7522               	; 	' the run cycle and did not travel a pixel. He stood at the foot of the
7523               	; 	' flight looking busy.
7524               	; 	'
7525               	; 	' TWO THINGS END THE RUN, AND BOTH ARE VISIBLE ON SCREEN.
7526               	; 	'
7527               	; 	' THE KOP ARRIVING ON THIS FLOOR OUTRANKS IT. This used to override the
7528               	; 	' flee as well as the climb, so for the whole of the run he held one
7529               	; 	' heading no matter where Kelly was -- and ran straight into him without
7530               	; 	' appearing to notice he was there. The flee above has already worked out
7531               	; 	' which way is away; clearing the run simply lets it stand.
7532               	; 	'
7533               	; 	' AND REACHING THE END WALL ENDS IT. It used to be a count of passes,
7534               	; 	' which expired somewhere in the middle of a floor and turned him round
7535               	; 	' for no reason the player could see. Running until the wall means every
7536               	; 	' change of direction has something on screen behind it.

8067 D6D2 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
8068 D6D4 0450  20 	b *r0
8069               	;
8070               	; 	' ======================================================================
8071               	; 	' COLLISIONS -- pure arithmetic against RAM, no VDP reads
8072               	; 	' ======================================================================
8073               	; 	' Kelly's box is [kfh, kfh + kh) above the slab; an obstacle's is
8074               	; 	' [ohb, oht). The ball's inset hitbox is what makes 8..10 avoidable both
8075               	; 	' ways -- without it there is a height at which the ball can be neither
8076               	; 	' jumped nor ducked, and an unavoidable hazard is not difficulty.
8077               	; 	' ONE HIT PER CONTACT, WITH A REFRACTORY PERIOD.
8078               	; 	'
8079               	; 	' The overlap test is true on every frame the player is touching
8080               	; 	' something, so with no latch at all one clumsy cart charges nine seconds
8081               	; 	' several times over -- and what the player sees is the clock jumping by
8082               	; 	' 27, from which the only available conclusion is that the penalty is
8083               	; 	' broken.
8084               	; 	'
8085               	; 	' A latch that clears on the first non-overlapping frame is still not
8086               	; 	' enough, and the case that breaks it is the BOUNCING BALL: it rises off
8087               	; 	' the player between bounces, which is a genuine loss of contact, so a
8088               	; 	' player standing still under one is taxed on every single bounce while
8089               	; 	' being given no opportunity to do anything about it. Technically two
8090               	; 	' collisions; in play, one situation.
8091               	; 	'
8092               	; 	' So obht() is a countdown, not a flag. A hit sets it to HITREF; it ticks
8093               	; 	' down only while the player is CLEAR, and the same obstacle cannot charge
8094               	; 	' again until it reaches zero. Contact holds the count up, so parking
8095               	; 	' inside something never earns a second penalty either.
8096               	; coll_obst:
8097               cvb_COLL_OBST
8098               	; 	IF klst = ST_ESC THEN RETURN		' riding is invincible, per the
8099 D6D6 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLST,r0
     D6D8 2186

     D6DC 0300

8603               	; 	' walking off the far RIGHT and arrested him across the whole store.
8604               	; 	'

8672               	; 	' the highest-numbered sprites on an over-full scanline, so the lowest
8673               	; 	' slots are the ones that can never disappear, and the player is the one
8674               	; 	' thing that must never disappear.
8675               	; 	'
8676               	; 	' THE BAND SPRITES SHARE A y ON PURPOSE. Slots 0 and 1 both sit at ky and
8677               	; 	' between them cover rows 0-15; slot 2 sits at ky+16. Boxes 0/1 and box 2
8678               	; 	' never share a scanline, so an actor costs at most TWO boxes on any line
8679               	; 	' -- and the VDP counts boxes, not pixels, so an empty overlap would have
8680               	; 	' cost just as much as a full one. Two actors meeting is four, exactly
8681               	; 	' the per-line limit, which is why obstacles are suppressed on Harry's
8682               	; 	' floor rather than merely being a kindness.

8806               	; 		' FOUR RUN FRAMES FROM TWO BITS of the animation counter, and no
8807               	; 		' divide -- `/` compiles to a real TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A) and
8808               	; 		' this is per-frame code. Two adjacent bits give 0,4,8,12.
8809               	; 		'
8810               	; 		' KELLY USES BITS 2 AND 3 AND HARRY USES 3 AND 4, because a stride
8811               	; 		' has to cover about a stride's worth of ground or the figure
8812               	; 		' skates. Kelly runs 4 px a frame, so four frames a pose is 16 px;
8813               	; 		' Harry runs 1.75, so eight frames a pose is 14. Matching the two
8814               	; 		' rates to the two speeds is what keeps both looking like running.

9499 E2AC C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
9500 E2AE 0450  20 	b *r0
9501               	;
9502               	; 	' ======================================================================
9503               	; 	' THE SCANNER
9504               	; 	' ======================================================================
9505               	; 	' Rows 21-23, 16 chars wide, centred: 48 characters whose PATTERNS are
9506               	; 	' poked directly, so a moving dot costs two writes and no name-table
9507               	; 	' traffic. With the flip (DESIGN.md 2a) this is how you know where Harry
9508               	; 	' is at all -- he can be one pixel off-screen and completely invisible.
9509               	; 	'
9510               	; 	' Rows 21-23 all sit in the THIRD screen third, so every scanner pattern
9511               	; 	' is at base 4096 and no third-selection arithmetic is needed.
9512               	; 	'
9513               	; 	' BOTH DOTS ARE WHITE and Kelly's BLINKS. The manual wants Kelly black
9514               	; 	' and Harry white, but this is the TMS9918's two-colours-per-8x1-cell
9515               	; 	' mode: a black dot and a white dot on the same pixel row of the same
9516               	; 	' character cannot both exist. Blinking distinguishes them with one
9517               	; 	' colour, which is what RallyX does with its player dot.
9518               	; 	' THE STEPS MOVE, AND EVERY ESCALATOR ON SCREEN MOVES WITH THEM. A flight
9519               	; 	' is twelve characters -- six per direction, sliced out of a rendered
9520               	; 	' 64x40 bitmap by genart.py -- so rewriting those twelve PATTERNS animates
9521               	; 	' the lot: 96 bytes, no name-table traffic, and the same cost whether one
9522               	; 	' flight is visible or ten. The phases slide the cells ALONG the slope, so
9523               	; 	' the steps travel up the flight rather than drifting sideways.
9524               	; esc_tick:
9525               cvb_ESC_TICK
9526               	; 	' ONLY SCREENS 0 AND 7 CARRY A FLIGHT, so six screens in eight skip this
9527               	; 	' entirely -- which is what makes rewriting the cells every frame
9528               	; 	' affordable. Nested, not `klsc > 0 AND klsc < 7`: the 9900 backend
9529               	; 	' miscompiles a compare-AND-compare (CLAUDE.md 3A).
9530               	; 	IF klsc > 0 THEN
9531 E2B0 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     E2B2 2174     
9532 E2B4 0201  20 	li r1,256   ; 1*256
     E2B6 0100     
9533 E2B8 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
9534 E2BA 1402  14 	jhe cv745
9535 E2BC 0460  28 	b @cv744
     E2BE E2D4     
9536               cv745
9537               	; 		IF klsc < 7 THEN RETURN
9538 E2C0 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     E2C2 2174     
9539 E2C4 0201  20 	li r1,1792   ; 7*256
     E2C6 0700     
9540 E2C8 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
9541 E2CA 1A02  14 	jl cv747
9542 E2CC 0460  28 	b @cv746
     E2CE E2D4     
9543               cv747
9544 E2D0 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
9545 E2D2 0450  20 	b *r0
9546               cv746
9547               	; 	END IF
9548               cv744
9549               	; 	' ONE PHASE PER PASS, AND NEVER MORE. There are four phases covering an
9550               	; 	' 8 px period, so advancing by the frame delta ALIASES: at fdv 2 the
9551               	; 	' pattern flips between two positions half a period apart and the
9552               	; 	' direction stops being readable, and at fdv 3 the sequence runs 0,3,2,1
9553               	; 	' -- the staircase visibly runs BACKWARDS, carrying its steps down while
9554               	; 	' the rider goes up. That is the wagon-wheel effect, and no amount of
9555               	; 	' phase arithmetic fixes it: a 4-phase cycle can only be stepped by 1.
9556               	; 	'
9557               	; 	' So the ANIMATION is the clock here, and the rider is paced from it --
9558               	; 	' see move_kelly, which advances him by the same fixed 2-and-1 per pass
9559               	; 	' rather than by fdv. Locking the two together is the only way they
9560               	; 	' cannot drift, and it is what "the player moves at the rate the steps
9561               	; 	' carry him" actually means. The cost is that a ride takes 36 passes
9562               	; 	' rather than 36 frames, so it slows down when the loop does -- which is
9563               	; 	' the honest behaviour for a machine that is carrying you.
9564               	; 	escp = escp + 1
9565 E2D4 D020  34 	movb @cvb_ESCP,r0
     E2D6 20B2     
9566 E2D8 0220  22 	ai r0,256	; 1
     E2DA 0100     
9567 E2DC D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_ESCP
     E2DE 20B2     
9568               	; 	IF escp > 3 THEN escp = 0
9569 E2E0 0201  20 	li r1,1024   ; 4*256
     E2E2 0400     
9570 E2E4 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
9571 E2E6 1402  14 	jhe cv749
9572 E2E8 0460  28 	b @cv748
     E2EA E2F2     
9573               cv749

     E2F0 20B2     
9576               cv748
9577               	; 	' ONLY THE CELLS THAT MOVE, AND ONLY THIS SCREEN'S. genart.py measures
9578               	; 	' which cells move -- the step cells and the COMPOSITE copies of them
9579               	; 	' that cross a floor -- and groups them WEST then EAST, because only one
9580               	; 	' direction is ever on screen: screen 0 carries a west flight and screen
9581               	; 	' 7 an east one.
9582               	; 	'
9583               	; 	' THIS IS THE ONLY PER-PASS WORK UNIQUE TO THESE TWO SCREENS, and it is
9584               	; 	' `define_char`, which in bitmap mode is LDIRVM3 -- the TRIPLE copy, once
9585               	; 	' per screen third. It therefore costs three times what the character
9586               	; 	' count suggests, which is why these screens run slower than the rest.
9587               	; 	'
9588               	; 	' THE WEST SCREEN USED TO COST HALF AS MUCH AGAIN, because it carries TWO
9589               	; 	' flights: floor 1's crosses into a shop floor and floor 3's into the
9590               	; 	' roof, so it needed both composite sets -- nine characters against the
9591               	; 	' east's six, 216 bytes a pass against 144. It measured 20 passes a
9592               	; 	' second where the east screen managed 24-26. The two sets are
9593               	; 	' PIXEL-IDENTICAL and are never wanted in the same screen THIRD, so they
9594               	; 	' share their characters now and are told apart by the per-third colour
9595               	; 	' table (see esc_deck_col in setup). Six either side.
9596               	; 	'
9597               	; 	' The handrail and the frame sit past the end of both ranges and never

9853 E52E C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
9854 E530 0450  20 	b *r0
9855               	;
9856               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------- the scanner's furniture
9857               	; 	' Without this the scanner is two dots in a void: you can see THAT Harry is
9858               	; 	' somewhere, but not what he is near, which is the one thing you actually
9859               	; 	' need when he is off-screen. Floor lines say which level, the slashes say
9860               	; 	' where that floor's escalator is, and the bar says where the elevator is --
9861               	; 	' exactly the three things the manual lists.
9862               	; 	'
9863               	; 	' FURNITURE AND DOTS ARE SEGREGATED BY PIXEL ROW ON PURPOSE, and a level
9864               	; 	' band is now FOUR rows, one per colour, because a pixel row carries
9865               	; 	' exactly one colour here:
9866               	; 	'
9867               	; 	'   +0  GREY    escalator head, and the elevator car
9868               	; 	'   +1  GREY    the air of that floor
9869               	; 	'   +2  GREY
9870               	; 	'   +3  YELLOW  the floor line -- and NOTHING else goes here
9871               	; 	'
9872               	; 	' THE FIRST THREE ROWS ARE THE FLOOR'S AIR AND EVERYTHING LIVES IN THEM:
9873               	; 	' the escalator's three-step diagonal, the lift car, and both actors, all
9874               	; 	' three pixels tall. They are grey in the table, and the Kop and the
9875               	; 	' crook recolour their own CHARACTER as they move (scan_mark) -- which is
9876               	; 	' what lets both of them fill the band instead of getting a pixel row
9877               	; 	' each. Eight pixels of this canvas is half a screen of the store, so
9878               	; 	' they are almost never in one character; when they are, they merge.
9879               	; 	'
9880               	; 	' Shrinking from six rows bought eight empty pixel rows, four above the
9881               	; 	' instrument and four below, so it no longer butts against the shop floor
9882               	; 	' above it or the bottom of the screen.
9883               	; 	'
9884               	; 	' The dots are erased by ANDing their bits out, so anything sharing a row
9885               	; 	' with one is rubbed away wherever an actor has passed -- which is why
9886               	; 	' scan_escs and scan_elev redraw every tick rather than once.
9887               	; scan_furn:
9888               cvb_SCAN_FURN

10160               	;
10161               	; 	' THE FLIGHT IS BLACK, and it has to be written per character because the
10162               	; 	' colour table only knows rows -- the same reason the two actors carry
10163               	; 	' their own colour (scan_mark).
10164               	; 	'
10165               	; 	' This runs BEFORE the markers every tick, which is the whole ordering
10166               	; 	' the radar needs: the lift car is painted first and the flights next, so
10167               	; 	' a Kop or a crook standing on either of them paints over it. Nothing
10168               	; 	' moves aside to make room -- the pixels are simply ORed together and the
10169               	; 	' last colour written wins, so an actor in the lift shaft shows as an
10170               	; 	' actor rather than shifting anywhere.
10171               	; scan_escc:

10433               	;
10434               	; 	' Blank the canvas by WRITING zeros rather than copying 384 of them out
10435               	; 	' of ROM. This runs twice a game -- at setup and on a new Krook -- so a
10436               	; 	' loop costs nothing anybody can perceive, and it hands the fixed area
10437               	; 	' back the best part of half a kilobyte, which is the budget that
10438               	; 	' actually binds. Paced in bursts: a few hundred VDP writes in a single
10439               	; 	' frame are silently dropped (CLAUDE.md 3A).

10510               	; 	sct = 0
10511 EA1A 04C0  14 	clr r0
10512 EA1C D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_SCT
     EA1E 21C5     
10513               	;
10514               	; 	' THE TWO ACTORS ARE SPRITES; EVERYTHING ELSE IS CHARACTERS.
10515               	; 	'
10516               	; 	' That split is what makes the instrument simple. The floor lines, the
10517               	; 	' flights and the lift car do not move relative to the canvas, so they
10518               	; 	' are pixels in the scanner's own characters and cost nothing to keep.
10519               	; 	' The Kop and the crook DO move, and as sprites they need no colour
10520               	; 	' table, no erase and no priority arithmetic -- they draw over the
10521               	; 	' furniture because sprites always do, and nothing shifts to make room.
10522               	; 	'
10523               	; 	' IT ALSO REMOVED THE BLINK. Kelly flashed because a pixel row of this
10524               	; 	' canvas can only carry one colour, so at close range the two markers
10525               	; 	' merged and the blink was the only thing left to tell them apart. Two
10526               	; 	' sprites are two colours wherever they stand.
10527               	; 	'
10528               	; 	' The furniture is drawn ONCE, by scan_furn. It used to be redrawn every
10529               	; 	' tick because erasing a character-based marker ANDed bits out of
10530               	; 	' whatever it was standing on; there is nothing left to rub it away. The

     EFA0 AA16     
11193               	;
11194               	; 	' THE BONUS IS COUNTED OUT, NOT AWARDED. Dropping the whole sum into the
11195               	; 	' score in one frame tells the player a number; ticking the clock down a
11196               	; 	' unit at a time, with the score climbing and a beat under each step,
11197               	; 	' tells them what the number was FOR. It is the pay-off for every second
11198               	; 	' they saved, and every arcade game of this era spends a moment on it.
11199               	; 	'
11200               	; 	' It also costs nothing to get right: the loop already has to run down
11201               	; 	' `tsec`, so the animation IS the calculation, and the repeated-addition
11202               	; 	' note below stops applying -- each step adds one band's worth.
11203               	; 	'
11204               	; 	' x100 for Krooks 1-9, x200 for 10-15, x300 from 16. Two independent
11205               	; 	' sources agree on those bands; the manual's own wording suggests
11206               	; 	' 1-8 / 9-16 / 17+ and DESIGN.md 0 records the disagreement.
11207               	; 	'
11208               	; 	' #bval MUST BE 16-BIT: 300 does not fit in a byte, and a plain variable
11209               	; 	' would silently truncate it to 44 (CLAUDE.md 3A).
11210               	; bonus_count:
11211               cvb_BONUS_COUNT
11212               	; 	#bval = 100
11213 EFA2 0200  20 	li r0,100
     EFA4 0064     
11214 EFA6 C800  38 	mov r0,@cvb__BVAL
     EFA8 20FE

11216 EFAA D020  34 	movb @cvb_KRK,r0
     EFAC 20B3

     EFB0 0A00

     EFB8 EFC2     
11221               cv866
11222 EFBA 0200  20 	li r0,200
     EFBC 00C8

     EFC0 20FE

11257               	; 	' One tick per unit, with its own note-off. Channel 2, which the effect
11258               	; 	' table uses for the upper voice of a two-note effect, so this cannot
11259               	; 	' cancel a sustained tone on channel 1 (CLAUDE.md 3A: two SOUNDs on one
11260               	; 	' channel back to back just cancel the first).
11261               	; 	SOUND 2,300,13

     F088 EFDA     
11313               	;
11314               	; 	' `calc_bonus` USED TO LIVE HERE and computed the whole sum by repeated
11315               	; 	' addition, to dodge two hazards at once: `tsec * bmul` is an 8-bit
11316               	; 	' product (a 50-unit capture in the x300 band pays 15,000, six times past
11317               	; 	' a byte), and multiplying properly lands on the MPY/r0 hazard where a
11318               	; 	' 16-bit variable read straight after being multiplied returns the
11319               	; 	' product's high word. Counting the bonus out a unit at a time (above)
11320               	; 	' removes the multiply entirely rather than working around it -- the loop
11321               	; 	' the player watches IS the arithmetic.
11322               	; 	'
11323               	; 	' It also fixed the last-tick case for free: `bn_loop` tests `tsec = 0`
11324               	; 	' on entry, where the old `FOR bi = 1 TO tsec` would have run its body
11325               	; 	' once and paid a full multiplier for no time at all (CLAUDE.md 3A).
11326               	;
11327               	; do_escape:
11328               cvb_DO_ESCAPE
11329               	; 	escapd = 0

     F1B8 AA16     
11461               	;
11462               	; 	' Silence every channel FIRST. SOUND latches, and the per-channel decay
11463               	; 	' counters are not ticked inside this loop -- a tone still sounding when
11464               	; 	' the pause starts would hang for its whole duration, which is the classic
11465               	; 	' CVBasic sticky-audio failure.
11466               	; 	' EVERY CHANNEL OFF, AND THE DECAY COUNTERS WITH THEM. The counters are
11467               	; 	' ticked by the main loop, so anything still sounding when the loop stops
11468               	; 	' -- for a capture, a death, the bonus count -- sustains for as long as
11469               	; 	' whatever replaced the loop takes. That is where the long beep over the
11470               	; 	' bonus tally came from: not the tally's own note, which turns itself off
11471               	; 	' after every unit, but a footstep or a hit that was still ringing when
11472               	; 	' Harry was caught.
11473               	; snd_off:
11474               cvb_SND_OFF

11547               	; 	' `SOUND ch,f,0` the last tone sustains for ever. And the second argument
11548               	; 	' is a 10-BIT DIVISOR, max 1023: SMALLER IS HIGHER, so a rising sweep is
11549               	; 	' written as a falling number and anything over 1023 is silently masked
11550               	; 	' to an unrelated pitch.

11899               	;
11900               	; 	' The font stays in bank 0: it is small, and keeping one readable thing
11901               	; 	' unbanked makes a bank-selection mistake obvious (text survives, art does
11902               	; 	' not) instead of producing a uniformly blank screen.
11903               	; 	INCLUDE "font.bas"
11904               	; 	' Keystone Kapers font -- GENERATED by assets/genfont.py. Do not edit; regenerate.
11905               	; 	'
11906               	; 	' Characters 32-90 (59 of them), 8 bytes each = 472 bytes. Williams' cabinet face:
11907               	; 	' heavy, squared, flat terminals, no serifs -- drawn 5x7 inside the 8x8 cell so a
11908               	; 	' blank column at the right and a blank row at the bottom keep it legible.
11909               	; 	'
11910               	; 	' Loaded with DEFINE CHAR 32,59,font_bits. The run must be CONTIGUOUS, so slots
11911               	; 	' with no glyph emit blanks rather than being skipped -- a gap would shift every
11912               	; 	' letter after it.
11913               	;
11914               	; font_bits:
11915               cvb_FONT_BITS
11916               	; 	DATA BYTE $00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00	' space

12035               	; 	' EVERYTHING BELOW THIS LINE IS ASSEMBLED INTO BANK 1, so the INCLUDE order

12047               	; 	INCLUDE "art.bas"
12048               	; 	' Keystone Kapers art -- GENERATED by assets/genart.py. Do not edit; regenerate.
12049               	; 	'
12050               	; 	' EVERY SPRITE IS 16x16: sprite size and magnification are GLOBAL on the
12051               	; 	' TMS9918, so small objects are drawn small INSIDE a 16x16 box rather than
12052               	; 	' being small sprites.
12053               	; 	'
12054               	; 	' EVERY 8-PX OBJECT SITS IN ROWS 8-15 OF ITS BOX, ducked Kelly included, so
12055               	; 	' one rule places them all: bottom edge = y + 16, and a thing resting on the
12056               	; 	' floor is at y = FLOORY - 16.
12057               	; 	'
12058               	; 	' Sprite bytes are QUADRANT-ORDERED: 16 rows of the left half, then 16 of
12059               	; 	' the right. Every block is an EVEN number of bytes -- an odd DATA BYTE run
12060               	; 	' silently misaligns every word table after it (CLAUDE.md 3A).
12061               	;
12062               	; spr_kelly:	' Kelly: RIGHT hat/face/tunic/tunic-arm-up/duck-hat/duck-face, then the same LEFT (+24), then the FOUR shared run frames. Patterns 0..60
12063               cvb_SPR_KELLY

12784               	;
12785               	; 	' -------------------------------------------------- scanner canvas
12786               	; 	' Chars 160-207: 16 cols x 3 rows over screen rows 21-23. Blank to start;
12787               	; 	' dots are plotted by VPOKEing the PATTERN table (rows 21-23 sit in the
12788               	; 	' third screen third, so every scanner pattern is at base 4096).
12789               	;
12790               	; 	' --------------------------------------------------- font colours
12791               	; 	' Chars 32-90, EIGHT bytes each. Without this the font keeps whatever
12792               	; 	' CVBasic left in the colour table, which on a green store made the HUD
12793               	; 	' unreadable.
12794               	;
12795               	; scan_col3:	' one 8-byte block per canvas row: 4 px of GREY margin, then a 3 px band of furniture GREY and a yellow floor line, per 4 px level, on dark green. The Kop and the crook are coloured PER CHARACTER at run time -- see scan_set

12820               	;
12821               	; 	' code map, for the source to reference:

12874               	; 	'   148 PARAP
12875               	; 	'   149 WALL
12876               	; 	'   150 KOPIC
12877               	; 	'   151 EXITC
12878               	; 	'   152 BAG
12879               	; 	'   153 CASE
12880               	; 	'   154 ROOFS
12881               	; 	'   155 EDHALF
12882               	; 	'   156 ENDWALL
12883               	; 	'   157 SCANBK
12884               	; 	'   158 SLABP
12885               	; 	INCLUDE "store.bas"
12886               	; 	' Keystone Kapers store -- GENERATED by assets/genstore.py. Do not edit.
12887               	; 	'
12888               	; 	' EIGHT band templates of 5 rows x 32 cols (160 bytes each) plus a 32-byte
12889               	; 	' index over (level, screen). The whole 8-screen, 4-level building is about
12890               	; 	' 1.3 KB instead of the 5,120 bytes it would cost to store literally.
12891               	; 	'
12892               	; 	' All eight templates are ONE contiguous block under ONE label, so a band
12893               	; 	' blit is
12894               	; 	'     SCREEN stor_tpl, tpl*160, toprow*32, 32, 5, 32
12895               	; 	' with no label ladder -- SCREEN needs a literal label and a label cannot be
12896               	; 	' chosen by a variable, which is why RallyX needed a 4-way IF over its maze
12897               	; 	' tables. One block sidesteps it.
12898               	; 	'
12899               	; 	' 160 is EVEN, so this cannot leave the location counter odd and misalign
